## Head decision intent and round approval — October 27

Local 71 connects visible head-decision drafts with approval for the same event/actor/round. A material edited or attempted decision that differs from current authoritative fingerprint, actor, complete order, shared-place value or raw explanation blocks that round’s approval. Save or explicitly discard the changes first; Review [group] draft focuses the exact existing editor legend. Discard decision changes restores current authoritative values in the same target. Existing 69 changed-target discard/review recovery remains explicit. Pristine forms and exact authoritative matches remain approval-ready, including a round.version increase and a still-waiting save callback. Returning values to the saved intent also removes the conflict without silently clearing the retained draft.

The reciprocal relationship pauses that round’s head-decision edits/save/discard while an approval callback is pending. Captured handlers consult synchronous per-round refs, preventing both edit→approval and approval→late-edit/save races before another render. Already-sent approvals are preserved. Failure, rejection or true without authoritative confirmation releases the callback wait with the existing 68 status-check guidance; this is not a new network-outcome reconciliation or indefinite lock. Other rounds, reading, navigation and unrelated controls retain their existing gates.

Material intents and pending approvals have per-instance ownership leases. Stable publishers update copied state Maps for rendering and separate refs for synchronous handlers; polling does not tear down an edited claim. Cleanup/end removes only the matching owner, so an old same-key instance cannot clear a newer claim. Only current visible eligible tie editors participate. A disappearing tie, eligibility/identity/round-generation change or navigation can unmount the form and end this local lifetime; no general draft persistence, cancellation, automatic retry/rebase or transport/server/schema change is introduced. Existing roles, slot/deadlock/shared restrictions, exact payloads, server CAS and immutable history remain unchanged.

The [dated evidence](LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md#october-27--head-decision-and-approval-intent-coordination-pass) executes actual LiveResults/StageReview using controlled callback/effect setup/dependency/unmount models and SSR. It does not establish real React StrictMode, DOM/focus/AT/phone/browser scheduling, backend acceptance or venue behavior. Source b61b2795378f227154983ffd4ddf76fec4faa536 and annotated before/source/delivery checkpoints preserve source, not data.

## Shared GET completion and connection-status ordering — October 27

Local64 gives successful and unsuccessful GET /api/live reads a10,000ms asynchronous completion policy covering fetch headers, JSON body consumption and63 structural parsing. It is2.5 times the normal4-second polling interval: slow reads can finish, while a stalled read eventually reports The shared event took too long to respond. Retry the connection. The hook retains last-good data/local edits, returns null for that failed check, and can schedule the next normal poll. Each GET owns its AbortController; timeout rejection settles before best-effort abort, and the deadline timer clears on every completion path. An abort-ignoring late response cannot re-enter hook setters after the race settles. This is not a hard real-time bound on synchronous JSON/main-thread work or background-throttled timers, nor proof that every transport has released resources.

Concurrent reads stay independent. There is no cancellation-on-new-read or shared-promise policy that could starve a user-triggered preflight. Each caller gets its own validated response or null; it does not borrow another read's result. Only connection error reporting/clearing follows the highest completed request sequence, so an older failure cannot overwrite a newer successful check, and an older success cannot clear a newer error. Every validated success still goes through the existing version/submission/cell merge, including an older-started response carrying newer authoritative data. A pending newer read does not suppress a completed older result. The existing event CAS remains authoritative for subsequent mutations.

POST requests return through their existing path before any GET controller/timer is allocated. No mutation timeout, cancellation, acknowledgement or automatic retry policy changes. The63 read schema and merge implementation, scoring/submission/approval/publication/history/receipt rules remain unchanged. No new framework, dependency, provider, environment setting or body-size limit is added. Effect cleanup keeps its existing future-poll cancellation; this delivery does not isolate tokens or stop all in-flight hook work on unmount. Those require separate scope.

Source d98bdb788ed1c747cd21ce15d33f09fad6e01b62 and annotated before/source/delivery checkpoints preserve reviewable source, not data or preview activation. [Dated verification](LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md#october-27--shared-get-completion-and-status-ordering-pass) uses controlled clocks, deferred fetch/body promises and the actual client code with a hook model; virtual elapsed time is not physical-network/browser/phone/venue evidence. No actual service/API role cohort is repeated because server/read-schema inputs remain unchanged.

## Shared-view read validation and recovery — October 27

Local63 validates successful GET /api/live views inside the shared client before returning them. This covers useLive and direct organizer-home reads. Known required and optional display/merge structures must have the expected objects, arrays, strings, numbers, enum values and nullable fields. Malformed known structures are rejected with the existing generic ApiError502 connection-retry wording, without reflecting response bodies, tokens or parser diagnostics. The hook's existing catch returns null, retains trusted data and reports the error; a later valid read clears it and uses the existing cell/submission/round merge protections. No empty defaults are invented. Generic POST replies remain governed by their existing operation acknowledgements rather than being mistaken for full views.

The read guard is deliberately structural: it accepts incomplete preparation, empty assigned collections, legacy/stage-v1 and role-filtered organizer/head/judge views without requiring undisclosed organizer identities, compatible unknown additions, omitted optional fields, opaque restoredHistory, nullable context/public values and historical audit display labels. It validates known nested standings, marks, feeds, progress, review/publication history and related collections when present. It does not recalculate scores, authorize roles, enforce setup readiness/cross-record consistency, prove exact mutation receipts or add body/time limits. Unknown additive fields are not interpreted. Server permissions/source/approval rules and client merge semantics remain authoritative and unchanged.

Deterministic fake fetch/deferred-hook tests establish invalid state replacement/error clearing before the fix, first-read recovery, last-good/local-draft preservation and retry after it. Empty-object responses can cause wrong access presentation; role-present partial responses reach the actual desk's unsafe setup expression. No actual React DOM crash, unmount or lost-draft incident is claimed. [Dated verification](LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md#october-27--shared-view-read-validation-and-recovery-pass) distinguishes harness, actual fictional API role payloads and build evidence. Source ba659d86b2804af3e2ee9eb31a6099bf0a425981 has annotated before/source/delivery checkpoints; these are not data backups or owner-preview activation. Poll timeout/cancellation, token/unmount isolation and out-of-order lifecycle findings are outside63 and require fresh scope.

## Publication preparation reuse and no-op setup saves — October 27

Local61 treats equality of the validated, server-revision-normalized persisted setup as a publication no-op. The existing setup event-version guard, frozen assignments, access checks, stored setup/context writes and receipt/idempotency behavior still apply; automaticStages-only context changes do not create a new public source version. Genuine setup differences remain conservatively publication-invalidating, even where a field is not disclosed publicly. The comparison occurs after server-owned advancementRevision restoration, so returning a finalist count to an old value remains a genuine versioned review change.

Preparation never reuses an underReview publication. An edited event whose name, venue or date returns to its previously released value therefore gets an exact fresh prepared snapshot, with the highest released revision plus one and supersedes pointing to that release. Immutable old snapshot values, original approvals, public history and prior status notices remain intact. The new preparation receives no organizer/website-admin approval automatically. The existing current head/source checks, full-event exact organizer review, independent admin release and event CAS remain mandatory. Repeated unchanged preparation of a usable prepared/requested/queued/released record remains idempotent; the stale release/release-set receipt barrier is checked before any reuse. This also repairs historical underReview records left by earlier identical saves without clearing review or reviving approvals.

## Admin operation acknowledgements and current-event status — October 27

Local59 prevents a successful HTTP status from creating an admin session or claiming publication by itself. The shared [admin acknowledgement contract](lib/live/admin-ack.ts) binds version1, accepted:true, a fresh exact64-character nonce, action and digest of the full operation/target body. Credentials remain in the separate authorization header. The judging server validates a supplied adminRequest marker before its initial service write, and checks the selected PUBLIC event ID against the actual complete release set before queuing it. Operational competition IDs and public snapshot competition IDs intentionally differ. Legacy direct API calls without the marker keep their existing reply shapes and permissions.

Admin list, service and retry/release replies have separate required schemas. Unknown own fields (including prototype-named fields), coerced enum values, wrong operation/nonce/digest/state, missing counters and partial render records fail confirmation. List validation checks structural display fields and identity bindings, not scoring calculations. Reads require JSON, fatal UTF-8 decoding and actual byte limits:8MiB for full historical lists and4KiB for action acknowledgements. Body reading has a2-second deadline in addition to the request timeout, with best-effort cancellation on rejection. No raw response error/body is reflected to users. Valid data beyond the list bound fails as an unconfirmed check rather than appearing empty; pagination is not implemented by59.

An invalid list acknowledgement cannot create a session/cookie or render an empty queue. A service result must confirm the requested state; retry wording reports the global delivery check rather than publication of an event. After release, the admin makes a fresh nonce-bound list request and checks the selected event's complete latest ID/fingerprint set, current scope, review state and release evidence. Global pending counts and the older PublicStore object passed into the request handler are not selected-event publication proof. Changed/incomplete/under-review targets remain unconfirmed; queued approved targets explain pending sending. The GET review page rechecks current completion before displaying Published, and only internally generated notice text is accepted. Stale confirmed/pending notices are replaced when current context no longer supports them; arbitrary and legacy success claims in URL notice parameters are omitted.

Uncertain action responses say that an action may already have been saved and direct the admin back to the selected event to check status before retrying. Failed reads do not suggest an action occurred. Event context stays on fixed local recovery links, with no duplicate-approval instruction, credential reflection or new cookie. Existing CSRF/origin checks, form limits,8-hour session expiry, logout, separate admin authority and58 receipt/CAS/history protections remain. No production authentication, cookie-policy, account or layout redesign is included.

Update the judging authority before the results/admin adapter: old adapters accept the additional response field; a new adapter against an old authority fails closed, even with a valid access code. The sign-in message therefore asks that both local services be updated and running. Owner services are not activated by this delivery. A future hosted adapter still requires its separately scoped authorization and verified contracts.

Focused mock tests write no stores. The two actual API checks use fresh owned3001/3110 fixtures with explicit process/configuration/store preflight; no old fixture outputs are reused. All test/build default outputs must be inspected and either isolated or backed by verified preimages before launch. See LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md for source/build correspondence, preservation proof and limits. Source checkpoints do not restore databases or reverse publication.

## Exact publication acknowledgements and uncertain-delivery recovery — October 27

Local58 implements the shared protocol in [delivery-ack.ts](lib/live/delivery-ack.ts). An acknowledgement has exactly version:1, accepted:true, deliveryId, kind and envelopeDigest. The digest covers the complete existing serialized three-field envelope, including ordered release-set members or status scope. Both sender dispatch paths require every expected field before recording delivery, release or synchronization. JSON responses are bounded to1024 actual bytes, fatal UTF-8 decoding and2seconds of body reading, in addition to the request deadline. Unknown fields/version, malformed/missing/oversized/partial/wrong acknowledgements and transport failure retain the same pending delivery ID; rejected bodies are best-effort cancelled and receiver text is not exposed.

The receiver acknowledges only after its existing file/receipt commit, or identically on an exact stored signature replay. Existing receipt strings, immutable snapshots and supersedes links are preserved without a migration. Update receiver before sender: old senders still receive200 from the new receiver; a new sender paired with an old receiver returning{} remains pending. No loopback gate, role, scoring calculation or production adapter changes.

**Uncertain delivery after correction:** a receiver may have committed before its acknowledgement was lost. Invalidated release/set outbox rows remain stale and unresolved; preparation explains that the website admin must use Retry sending before updated results can be prepared. An unchanged queued preparation still reuses its exact ID/fingerprint. Authenticated receipt-only requests use X-Publication-Receipt-Only:1 on the existing local receive endpoint and the unchanged envelope/signature. They serialize behind the same receiver queue and never release, attest, persist or switch a public pointer. Exact stored receipt returns accepted:true; authoritative absence returns accepted:false with matching version/ID/kind/digest. Authentication errors, signature conflicts, malformed replies, outages and timeouts are unknown, never absence. After source invalidation, earlier in-process commits precede lookup; later new releases still require current-source attestation. This is a single-process fixture contract, not a hosted multi-worker or power-loss durability guarantee.

Retry reconciles stale rows using exact outbox/publication CAS and an event-version guard in one D1 batch. Exact presence records the original snapshot as released and Under review and queues any required status notice. Exact absence cancels only that delivery; it deletes no public history. Unknown leaves preparation blocked. The event-version advance prevents a previously loaded organizer action from crossing the cleared barrier with an old revision. Conflicting prepared/requested drafts at an already-published revision become stale history with their original timestamps; no approval is transferred. Fresh publication requires renewed exact head/organizer/admin approvals, revision=current+1 and the correct supersedes pointer. Normal acknowledgements complete only pending rows; they cannot clear stale barriers, downgrade delivered state or clear an existing Under review flag. Source invalidation compares the exact previously loaded publication data as well.

Suspension permits receipt reconciliation and status notices, while new releases remain blocked. Reconciliation currently requires an active event and valid organizer access: reactivate an archived event before the website admin retries its ambiguous delivery. Do not bypass archive/role guards or infer absence when access is unavailable. Admin success wording and production adapter work remain separate future scopes.

Focused fixtures require explicit isolated endpoints, a fresh LOCAL_TEST_RECEIVER_STORE, and LOCAL_TEST_D1_STORE pointing to the same owned run's d1 directory for the historical-draft seed. The publication API test refuses an existing restart-manifest destination before fixture mutations and writes exclusively; configure PUBLICATION_RESTART_FIXTURE_FILE to a fresh owned path. Preserve earlier files rather than deleting them to satisfy preflight. See LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md for source/checkpoints, verification and preservation limits. Source rollback does not restore data or undo a public commit.

## Production publication readiness and receipt confirmation — October 27

Review57 is source-only against clean main56daa8f, coordinated with Country Dance daily maintenance. It does not provision a publisher, open a hosted/owner page, run services/tests, change live data or retry denied lifecycle/cleanup commands. The October26 live judging milestone remains separate from a production publication service. Current scope is planning and one concrete local reliability follow-up; no provider/account capability is inferred from source review.

**First fix delivery confirmation.** lib/live/server.ts:886-896 and905-916 mark durable outbox delivery and released/synchronized publication status after response.ok alone. scripts/fake-publisher.mts:28,45 returns200 with an empty JSON object both after persisting and on exact replay. The receiver retains a deliveryId-to-serialized-message receipt and rejects changed retries, but the sender does not receive/validate a bound acknowledgement. Source review therefore identifies a possible false success on an unrelated or malformed200; no fault reproduction or data-loss claim is made by this review.

Next58 should define a small shared versioned acknowledgement containing accepted:true, exact deliveryId/kind and a digest of the complete serialized delivery envelope. Return it only after durable receipt/record commit and identically on an exact retry; derive it from existing receipt strings without deleting history. The sender must validate bounded JSON and every expected field before marking any release-set member or status delivered. Missing/invalid JSON, wrong ID/kind/digest, partial-set content, timeout and transport success without confirmation retain pending state and the same retry ID. Preserve stale-source handling, Under review notices, current CAS guards, independent approvals and existing receipts. Update the receiver first, then sender: old senders can accept the new response, while a new sender paired with an old empty-ack receiver remains pending. Document that conservative mixed-version behavior rather than silently accepting old empty responses. This is a proposed protocol until implementation evidence is recorded.

| Production concern | Existing source contract | Proposed local preparation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Approval and disclosure | buildPublicSnapshot in lib/live/publication.ts requires complete exact head-approved stages/Finals membership and allowlists public values with scoped IDs; organizer preparation/request is separate from owner release | Preserve exact fingerprints, independent admin identity/decision and author-only notes; public queries use a dedicated allowlisted projection |
| Durable release and history | db/schema.ts stores operational snapshots/outbox; ownerPublication uses guarded D1 batches, whole-event sets and ordered supersedes; local receiver retains old records/current pointers/receipts/notices | Extract pure protocol/store interfaces; model released payloads/current pointers/idempotency receipts/status notices separately from operational tables, with atomic compare-and-swap and append-only migrations |
| Runtime and authorization | localPublisher rejects nonloopback hosts; Node receiver uses fs/renameSync, an in-process queue and expiring in-memory admin sessions | Add a separately tested Worker-compatible storage/session/auth adapter; do not remove loopback gates or reuse organizer/judge capabilities as admin authentication |
| Route ownership | public-site renderer/admin use standalone-origin absolute links/assets; judging home already owns / | Candidate same-project public prefix and central URL builder preserve organizer routes; test all links/forms/redirects/assets/cookies under that prefix |
| Operations and recovery | local optional dispatcher retries approved work only; source tags cannot restore database state | Durable bounded retries, retry/lag visibility, failure/suspension behavior, consistent DB backup/restore proof and additive rollback compatibility before hosted activation |

Preserve the [confirmed service-continuity recommendation](#service-ownership-and-continuity--confirmed): separate public origin/deployment identity/public snapshot storage from operational D1, with independent owner-only publisher administration. Reuse current judging DB/outbox; prototype the independent public store/transport/session contracts locally before choosing a hosted service. A same-project prefix with separately named public/admin tables is an infrastructure-reduction alternative, not the approved/default production design: it shares a physical failure/security boundary and needs an explicit recorded boundary assessment before adoption. Logical independent admin authority is required in either design. No production admin identity/allowlist, session provisioning, domain/prefix configuration, scheduler or hosted store is currently verified/configured. These are genuine activation dependencies, not reasons to block the local receipt fix or adapter prototype. Route any necessary owner facts through MASTER MASTER CHATTER when their implementation stage needs them; do not invent identities or copy loopback secrets into production.

Staging: (1) exact acknowledgement and focused fictional failure/retry tests; (2) pure transport/store contracts with unchanged public rendering and legacy receipt compatibility; (3) isolated additive D1 public-store/session adapter, complete-event atomicity and prefix-aware routes; (4) local end-to-end publication/correction/status/race/restart-recovery tests with original/revised snapshots and privacy assertions. Compare provider-supported durability/authentication/deployment capabilities against current primary documentation before selecting or activating a hosted adapter. Each stage receives its own source checkpoint, verification and retained fixture evidence. Source rollback must not drop public receipts/revisions; a database restore requires a separately proved consistent backup. Production activation and any data migration remain outside this read-only review.

## Canonical document section reader — October 25

Initial source679a180 and closing-hash correction636e282 add dependency-free scripts/project-context.mjs and focused tests. The fixed aliases agents/spec/report select only AGENTS.md, PROJECT_SPEC.md and LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md relative to the script's checkout, independent of caller CWD. Non-regular files, invalid UTF-8, arbitrary document names, malformed flags/selectors and ambiguous headings fail with bounded help and no content dump. The CLI never writes canonical files or private configuration.

`node scripts/project-context.mjs spec list --search "dependency"` lists matching canonical ATX headings with actual1-based source lines and levels. `read --line N` reads that exact heading through the next same/higher-level heading, retaining nested sections. `read --heading "Exact heading"` requires a unique exact title; use a listed line for duplicates, empty titles or very long titles. The small scanner supports canonical `#` through `######` headings, optional closing hashes (including empty titles such as `# ##`, while retaining unseparated `name#`) and backtick/tilde fences (including length/character matching); it does not claim a general Markdown/Setext parser. The full original history and task map remain authoritative.

Each stdout page is at most12288 UTF-8 bytes, including source/hash, section source-line/byte range, selection-relative page range, framing, status and explicit NEXT command when truncated. Byte ends are exclusive. CONTENT declares the exact following payload byte count; END CONTENT framing is additional output, not source content. This framing preserves CRLF, Unicode/combining characters and long single lines without inserted source newlines, loss, overlap or broken UTF-8. NEXT binds document SHA256, selection and byte offset; changed documents, different selections and invalid boundaries require restarting the selection. Cursors are continuity guards, not credentials. Output is bounded per page; choose a task section and continue only when needed.

[Isolated evidence](LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md#october-25--canonical-document-section-reader-pass) records7 focused fixture tests, actual canonical invocations from outside the checkout, exact reconstructed bytes,12KiB bounds, unchanged source/private/config/generated hashes, type/lint results and limitations. [Preferred promotion](LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md#october-25--preferred-canonical-reader-promotion-pass) separately verifies the installed script, emitted PowerShell NEXT command, exact source payload and final mirror/data/serving checks. The reader changes no application/dependency/runtime behavior; its checkpoint preserves source rollback, not data.

## AJV-selected URI parser patch and bundled residual — October 25

Source40d96a5 on codex/fast-uri-package-patch selects fast-uri3.1.9 within AJV8.20.0's existing `^3.0.1` range. Only its lock resolution key/integrity, empty snapshot key and AJV parent reference change; package.json, workspace policy, parent versions, application code and embedded packages remain unchanged. The [isolated evidence](LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md#october-25--isolated-ajv-uri-parser-patch-pass) preserves commands, integrity/graph proof, tests and runtime limits; separate [preferred activation evidence](LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md#october-25--preferred-ajv-uri-parser-promotion-pass) verifies actual selected dependencies, local serving and data preservation after integration.

The physical dependency is RSC19.2.9's Webpack peer → webpack5.109.2 → schema-utils4.3.3 (also through minimizer-webpack-plugin5.6.1) → AJV8.20.0 → fast-uri. AJV's actual default resolver uses the new child. AJV6.15.0 and Next's inspected compiled schema-utils/conf implementations use uri-js instead. An application value resembling a URI is not itself an attacker-controlled schema `$id`/`$ref`. The inspected schema-utils consumer compiles repository/vendor schemas synchronously without a network loader; project SSRF/XSS or arbitrary-schema reachability has not been established.

The [Fastify3.1.9 security release](https://github.com/fastify/fast-uri/releases/tag/v3.1.9), current [maintainer advisories](https://github.com/fastify/fast-uri/security/advisories), [official registry metadata](https://registry.npmjs.org/fast-uri/3.1.9) and [AJV resolver contract](https://ajv.js.org/options.html#uriresolver) were checked October25. All ten external3.1.5 advisory-ID/package/version records disappear with no additions or changes to other records;16 remain (11high/4moderate/1low), and audit still exits1. The13 focused tests cover malformed-host/scheme/port/reference cases and ordinary AJV/schema-utils compatibility without network access. Pure parser examples are not a demonstrated application exploit.

Passive Node module-load observation during the normal build and isolated normal-plugin development home GET recorded no target package loads. The87 output JS files/222 parsed import edges contain no identified parser markers or target imports; no source maps were generated. This is bounded observed non-loading/output inspection, not proof of global unreachability, every possible plugin path, or hosted behavior. Direct consumer tests separately exercise the actual AJV-selected implementation.

Playwright-core1.64.0 `lib/utilsBundle.js` embeds another fast-uri/AJV implementation. Its [official release lock](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/playwright/v1.64.0/package-lock.json) records3.1.7, which is an origin lead rather than an exact embedded-version fingerprint. Actual bundled canonicalizeHost still adopts WHATWG hostname without the later whole-host consumption guard. Exported MCP Client/Server validators compile tool output and requested-form schemas; no current project invocation or exploit was established. The bundle hash remains75F1431EA7EE00B2B291A0A72D4FA1C07AE04D999F21A00CD03B8F454A5840FC. Updating the external child does not patch this embedded implementation, which is not represented by the ten removed npm records. Keep its review separate; do not patch node_modules or presume that a parent update fixes it.

Registry checks found latest stable Miniflare 4.20260730.0 still using Undici 7.28.0; current Wrangler 4.152.0/Cloudflare Vite 1.65.0 move to Miniflare 5.20261019.2-beta. This batch avoids that wider prerelease-major toolchain transition. Undici 7.30.1's Node >=20.18.1 engine is met by the current Node 24 runtime.

The 24 npm audit records all reference 7.24.8 through the external Miniflare path. Their stated fixed-line groups are seven at 7.28.0, five at 7.29.0, nine at 7.29.1 and three at 7.30.1. Current primary [Undici advisories](https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories) and [7.30.1 release](https://github.com/nodejs/undici/releases/tag/v7.30.1) were checked alongside the audit. The focused reproductions cover [encoded cookie delimiters](https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-p88m-4jfj-68fv), [SameSite substring matching](https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-g8m3-5g58-fq7m) and [case-sensitive SRI bypass](https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-f72v-6w45-q472). These have feature/input prerequisites; installed vulnerable versions and severity counts do not establish an application exploit. Six pure tests follow Cloudflare Vite → Miniflare → actual npm package, use only in-memory cookie/data-URL fixtures, and preserve valid cookie/integrity behavior.

## Image parser security patch — October 24

Source 3b00fb6 on codex/image-size-security addresses the two image-size infinite-loop findings with one parent/version-specific resolution override. Vinext 1.0.0-beta.3 declares exactly image-size 2.0.2, so an ordinary lockfile refresh cannot choose a fixed patch. pnpm-workspace.yaml now maps only vinext@1.0.0-beta.3>image-size to 2.0.3 using [pnpm's documented parent selector](https://pnpm.io/settings/dependency-resolution#overrides). No direct dependency, unrelated update, broad override, framework replacement or build-policy change is included. Remove/reassess the override when upgrading Vinext or when its declared dependency supports a fixed parser. The 13-line lock diff changes only that edge, package integrity/engine and snapshot; package.json is unchanged. Node 24 satisfies the new parser's Node >=18 engine.

[The reviewed JXL/HEIF advisory](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-5p2g-fcmc-qvqq) and [ICNS advisory](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-w3rx-r6r6-pgpr) list 2.0.3 as patched. Fresh publisher registry metadata confirms that version exists and points to the moved Codeberg upstream; the older unpublished-version reports are stale. The [original researcher disclosure](https://joshua.hu/image-size-infinite-loop-dos-vulnerabilities) explains non-advancing entries. Codeberg pages were unavailable through the web tool; the published package source and actual parser behavior were inspected instead. Vinext's installed code parses repository static/metadata image buffers. This is a build/development input-hardening patch; no attacker-controlled remote upload path or hosted exploit was established. A framework-maintainer comment about newer releases is not substituted for inspection of this installed beta.

Five focused regressions resolve the parser through Vinext's actual import location. Valid final baseline tests with 2.0.2 time out separately for zero-length ICNS, HEIF and JXL entries; each is isolated from the test runner in a disposable child. With 2.0.3 all three reject promptly. The committed child guard also caps V8 heap at 64 MiB and execution at 1.5 seconds. Repository JPEG dimensions (150×150, 640×449, 640×480), the 1200×630 social image, and Vinext's actual metadata entry generation retain exact dimensions/bytes. Both require/CJS and framework ESM paths resolve the same published package version; no vendored image-size copy was found in the inspected Vinext deps path. This verifies selected package behavior, not every malformed image or runtime exposure condition.

Frozen installation, production build, TypeScript and focused lint pass. Full lint retains the same three reviewed navigation warnings and zero errors. The fresh audit's advisory-ID/package/version set removes exactly the two image-size records, adds none, and leaves all others unchanged: 50 records (0 critical, 23 high, 19 moderate, 8 low). Existing residual findings are not suppressed or declared fixed.

Compiled synthetic smoke checks preserve five JPEG response bodies byte-for-byte, including /og, and app/receiver rendering at 360/1440 with loaded images, no page errors or overflow. Opening a fresh app context automatically performs sample create/setup writes in its own new D1 store; the evidence records those two actions and does not claim zero writes. No score, approval or publication action occurred. Fresh ignored configuration/manifest/stores live under the retained worktree's work/image-size-check, separate from prior synthetic evidence and all owner state. Only proved 3001/3110 process trees were stopped after checks. The [report](LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md#october-24--isolated-image-parser-security-patch) records evidence and the pending master promotion. No full prior 51-unit/48-API/38-browser rehearsal was repeated for this isolated parser edge. Owner walkthrough, original/corrected records, scores/notes/history, 3000/3100 and the live site remain outside the worker's changes.

The master subsequently installed the exact frozen lockfile in the preferred checkout, passed the five parser regressions and a fresh production build, then restarted only the proved owner preview processes. All three owner routes returned HTTP 200, original/corrected values stayed 4.5000/5.4167, and all 16 business tables plus full publication/configuration hashes were unchanged. Quiescent state copies and the old physical parser package were retained and hash-verified; no full restoration was performed. The master promotion checkpoint is checkpoint/local-image-parser-promotion-2026-10-24. Details and source-versus-data rollback limits are in the operator record; the live site remains untouched.

Line addresses refer to each exact published dist/src/index.js, with the installed sealed May file as baseline. These are source findings, not runtime PASS results. All maintained fetchers still return runTransform without await inside their local try. Published documentation limits are not proof of identical enforcement in this local mock. No memory/performance/security guarantee is inferred.

The omitted-format finding has a narrower contract than assumed in the rehearsal narrative. [Official Images binding documentation](https://developers.cloudflare.com/images/optimization/binding/#outputoptions) requires a format and specifies no default; the installed workers-types ImageOutputOptions.format is also mandatory (13692–13704). All three maintained consumers still assign JPEG MIME without invoking jpeg() in their fallback. ## Sharp copy-owned compatibility rehearsal — October 27

Bounded 75 independently installs Miniflare 4.20260515.0 with a scoped Sharp 0.35.5 override in a minimal synthetic dependency testbed. It deliberately crosses the parent's declared ^0.34.5 range. This is a compatibility experiment, not an app dependency upgrade, exploit assessment or hosted remediation. The existing app manifest, lockfile, workspace policy, installed packages and Miniflare source remain unchanged. The retained and preferred installations still select Sharp 0.34.5 through Cloudflare and 0.35.5 through Next; the 74 app audit remains the dated 17-record baseline. Correct 74 inventory wording: **202 combined installed-file records, 101 in each root**, not 202 in each root.

The separately resolved testbed retains the existing Miniflare Undici 7.30.1 override and allowBuilds list; initial pnpm 11.25.0 install uses --ignore-scripts and --package-import-method=copy, with exclusive store, virtual store, configuration, cache, temporary, state and output paths. No existing node_modules is copied. Exact BigInt dev+ino, nlink, raw hashes and link-target checks precede native loading: 995 installed regular files all have nlink=1, with zero identity collisions against 224 existing file records (202 installed plus 22 consumer) or 925 own-store files. All 51 aliases resolve inside the owned testbed. The master independently verifies this isolation gate. No rebuild or lifecycle script is subsequently needed. Selected native execution reports Sharp 0.35.5, libvips 8.18.7, libheif 1.23.5 and librsvg 2.63.2; normal resolution selects workerd 1.20260515.1 and its copied Windows executable.

Actual public getImagesBinding RPC exercises unmodified imagesLocalFetcher, multipart File/ArrayBuffer input, native metadata, transformations and Duplex Response output in an owned standalone Worker on loopback 3001. Trusted inputs are 12×8 PNG/JPEG/WebP/AVIF/GIF/TIFF, a literal SVG and six malformed bytes; a separately constructed 1×1 six-channel TIFF verifies the channel limit without large allocations. Of 24 initial matrix checks, 22 pass and two fail: AVIF info rejects metadata.format=heif, and the expected malformed-transform rejection does not occur. Earlier progress messages incorrectly said 25/23; the raw 24/22 record governs. Nine follow-up observations explain the limits:

- Explicit PNG/JPEG/WebP/AVIF output MIME, decoded dimensions and streamed bodies work. All seven normal input formats transform to PNG. Rotation alone produces 8×12; contain produces a 20×20 canvas with measured padding and a nonblack center.
- AVIF decoding/transformation works, but info rejects heif as unsupported. TIFF info is also unsupported, while TIFF transformation works. These are measured candidate-consumer behaviors; no old-native comparison establishes which are regressions.
- Omitted output format advertises image/jpeg while returning unchanged PNG bytes. Malformed six-byte input and the valid six-channel TIFF both yield status 200/image/png with an empty body on transform, while info rejects with an internal 500 error. Rejection is not consistently propagated through the streamed response.
- Sharp's default and explicit limitInputChannels=5 reject the six-channel TIFF; explicit 6 accepts its metadata. The earlier raw-six-channel constructor rejection is a different validation and is not evidence for that decoder limit.

The candidate is **not cleared for promotion**. Next master step: choose a separately scoped consumer compatibility repair or maintained parent cohort, reproduce these exact cases and decide their required contract before application installation. Do not patch shared installed files or silently widen this experiment. An upgraded package alone does not resolve the consumer's status/MIME/metadata behavior. AVIF output bytes/default quality were not compared against old native execution, and this small fixture set does not establish performance, memory safety or venue/hosted behavior.

Fresh candidate audit reports two ws records (one high, one moderate), zero Sharp records; this minimal graph differs from the app graph and is not a 17→2 application remediation. Direct [ws memory-disclosure](https://github.com/websockets/ws/security/advisories/GHSA-58qx-3vcg-4xpx) and [fragment exhaustion](https://github.com/websockets/ws/security/advisories/GHSA-96hv-2xvq-fx4p) advisories confirm affected installed 8.18.0 and fixed 8.20.1/8.21.0 respectively. No WebSocket exploit, ws update or exposure claim is made. The [Sharp librsvg advisory](https://github.com/lovell/sharp/security/advisories/GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w) places the patched package threshold at 0.35.5; absence of its audit record is limited to the new resolution.

Child commands have 90-second parent timeouts, 1 MiB captured output limits and 192 MiB V8 heap settings; ordinary image outputs are capped at 64 KiB, inputs at 974 bytes, follow-up malformed/channel outputs are measured as zero. Node host maxRSS observations (including 145,516 KiB in the first runtime) exclude workerd; V8 heap is not a native/total RSS cap. The second runtime retains actual command lines, creation times, ancestry and loopback internal ports before RPC. The first matrix retains ready URL/configuration/PID/disposal evidence but lacks its own descendant/listener manifest; the second manifest does not prove the first process tree. Both Miniflare instances dispose normally; fresh 3001 checks and owned-process absence release the runtime claim without a kill. Owner services 3000/3100 are not probed, changed or stopped. No app API, owner store/event, native desktop/browser, full suite, build, deployment, global/policy or private-state change occurs.

Before/after seals preserve all 227 tracked inputs before documentation, 2,625 prior evidence files, 114 build files, six generated/private configuration preimages, all 202 combined installed-file records and 22 consumers; all candidate package files/links remain unchanged too. Only SPEC/REPORT receive inserted evidence. Prior report bytes and its first reading guide remain exact. Evidence, fixture failures, install/audit logs, process manifests, copied packages and lockfile remain in retained work/sharp-copy-compatibility-check. Source tags cannot restore dependency/data state. The master owns verification/integration, guarded mirror sync and exact-tip branch cleanup after delivery; no live release is implied. See [dated record](LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md#october-27--sharp-copy-owned-compatibility-rehearsal).

## Sharp native image residual assessment — October 27

Read-only 74 examines source aebbb3478508e95edd438237613f6263bfc03c83 and both Projects installations. Cloudflare Vite 1.37.1 and Wrangler 4.92.0 each pin Miniflare 4.20260515.0, whose declared ^0.34.5 resolves Sharp 0.34.5. Next 16.4.1 separately resolves Sharp 0.35.5; it does not replace the Miniflare copy. Each virtual store has these two Sharp versions and corresponding Windows x64 native packages. Packaged versions.json reports old libvips/heif/librsvg 8.17.3/1.20.2/2.61.2 versus 8.18.7/1.23.5/2.63.2 in the newer package; no native version query or decoder load is performed.

Fresh pnpm 11.25.0 audit returns 17 advisory records (11 high, 5 moderate, 1 low), including these three high Sharp records. Each reports 0.34.5 through the same three Cloudflare tree paths; paths are not independent physical copies or demonstrated exploits. Maintainer records were checked directly:

| Record · component | Affected → fixed Sharp range | Relevant prerequisite/limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [GHSA-f88m-g3jw-g9cj · libvips](https://github.com/lovell/sharp/security/advisories/GHSA-f88m-g3jw-g9cj) | <0.35.0 → >=0.35.0 | Untrusted image decoding; workaround names GIF/TIFF/VIPS loaders. |
| [GHSA-rgj7-g3m4-5g8c · libheif](https://github.com/lovell/sharp/security/advisories/GHSA-rgj7-g3m4-5g8c) | <0.35.4 → >=0.35.4 | HEIF/AVIF input; RCE statement includes glibc-Linux/runtime conditions. |
| [GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w · librsvg](https://github.com/lovell/sharp/security/advisories/GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w) | <0.35.5 → >=0.35.5 | SVG decoding; runtime-specific glibc-Linux RCE conditions. |

These are affected installed ranges, not a demonstrated Windows/application exploit or false-positive clearance. Normal package resolution selects the recorded Windows native candidate; custom process loaders/global library state and current listeners are unprobed. The RCE platform caveat does not establish absence of other memory-safety/availability risk.

Actual Miniflare imagesLocalFetcher lazily imports Sharp, decodes a multipart File ArrayBuffer with {}, reads metadata, applies rotation/contain resize, emits AVIF/JPEG/PNG/WebP and returns a Duplex response. Ordinary Images bindings/services require options.images, absent from the actual inline localBindingConfig (main/nodejs_compat/D1/R2); no wrangler.jsonc is assumed. However the generic internal HTTP loopback dispatcher selects that image service by its custom-service header without an Images-option check. Thus absent binding does not prove decoder unreachability. Its actual host/listener/exposure and caller prerequisites were not tested.

Installed Vinext beta.3's default dev/RSC paths validate and redirect source images; its Node production fallback serves source bytes. Worker optimization needs ASSETS plus a registered optimizer; generated adapter registration is a no-op without a configured adapter, and this source calls vinext() without one. No first-party next/image/optimizer consumer was found. The retained 114-file build has no native binary extension and no Sharp/Miniflare markers in 87 JavaScript/map files. This is scoped static evidence, not a full module trace, runtime exclusion or live-v5 assessment; package audit dev labels alone do not prove production absence.

Registry metadata currently reports latest-tag Miniflare 4.20260730.0 pinning Sharp 0.35.2, insufficient for the two later records. Published Vite plugin 1.65.0 and Wrangler 4.154.0 select different Miniflare 5 betas with Sharp 0.35.5; the plugin needs Wrangler ^4.152.0 and Wrangler's worker-types peer moves to ^5.20261023.1. The [Wrangler release](https://github.com/cloudflare/workers-sdk/releases/tag/wrangler@4.154.0) also changes the registry-path contract to CLOUDFLARE_DEV_REGISTRY_PATH; the current MINIFLARE_REGISTRY_PATH isolation requires migration verification. The [current API](https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/vite-plugin/reference/api/) still supports inline config; do not invent a required config file. Vinext 1.3.1 would require React/RSC ^19.3.1 and is unnecessary for this residual.

Recommended next candidate: a new master-scoped, synthetic-only independently installed/copy-owned Miniflare 4 + Sharp 0.35.5 compatibility rehearsal. This deliberately crosses the declared ^0.34.5 range and is not declared safe from source inspection. The used factory/ArrayBuffer/Duplex/metadata/resize/output APIs remain in newer source, but [Sharp 0.35 changes](https://github.com/lovell/sharp/blob/main/docs/src/content/docs/changelog/v0.35.0.md) include default limitInputChannels=5, AVIF output defaults and packaging changes. Node 24.22.1 meets the documented 20.9 floor; native ABI/codec/stream behavior remains untested. Future gates: verify independent regular-file ownership/link targets; preserve manifest/lock and usable dependency preimages; honor build policy; test exact consumer import/stream/metadata/formats and bounded malformed/high-channel behavior in owned processes; compare advisory/resolution evidence, appropriate regressions and one coherent build before master promotion. Do not hand-edit shared files or apply a blanket override/audit-fix.

Both node_modules roots/stores are real directories, but exact BigInt dev+ino confirms all six native DLL/addon counterparts share files (old link count 5/new 3). Of 101 logical file pairs, 95 are raw-equal and 62 share exact identity; six differing files are checkout-specific generated semver shims. All 202 per-root file hashes remain unchanged. Original Number-inode metadata rounded 54 records and is historical; corrected BigInt evidence governs identity. Source checkpoints cannot restore dependencies or data. The wider coherent Cloudflare cohort is an alternative requiring registry/storage/config/RSC/D1 compatibility work. If rehearsal is deferred, the master retains this installed residual and triggers review on a maintained parent declaring Sharp >=0.35.5 or before image input/optimizer/exposure changes. No remediation, native/test/build/service/hosted action is performed in 74. See [dated evidence](LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md#october-27--sharp-native-residual-assessment-pass).

## Dependency health and compatible security cohort — October 24

Current scoped Sharp paths/advisories/next action are in the [October 27 assessment](#sharp-native-image-residual-assessment--october-27). The cohort and aggregate counts below remain dated history; other residuals were not reassessed by that scoped pass.

From main/a337589, read-only pnpm 11.25.0 audit found 84 advisory records across 14 package names (5 critical, 36 high, 33 moderate, 10 low). Counts are advisory records, not independent exploitable app defects. The app runs Vinext/Vite and Cloudflare Workers, not the Next.js server. Current application writes use JSON /api/live; no application use-server/use-cache directives or next/image imports were found. This narrows investigation but does not establish framework endpoints or decoders unreachable: Vinext has an independent /_next/image implementation, development enables server-action handling, and progressive multipart decoding can precede action-function validation. No exploit against this application or hosted vulnerability was established.

Source c0ab5c0 on codex/dependency-security-cohort keeps Vinext 1.0.0-beta.3 and existing major versions. Next and eslint-config-next move 16.2.6 → 16.4.1; React, React DOM and react-server-dom-webpack move 19.2.6 → 19.2.9; Vite moves 8.0.13 → 8.1.6. Installed package engines/peers support Node 24.19.0 and these same-major versions; frozen installation and the production build pass without peer-resolution errors. No permission/build-policy overrides, framework replacement, app source, schema or scoring change is included. Updated ESLint reports three reviewed warning-level full-document navigations (archive deletion and Events workspace), retained for their existing context-reset behavior.

The upstream cross-check mattered: the initial audit listed July RSC DoS, while the newer official October 14 advisories include [RSC RCE](https://github.com/react/react/security/advisories/GHSA-xqc2-xf4c-x3h3) and [RSC DoS](https://github.com/react/react/security/advisories/GHSA-h3gv-c5rc-p453), requiring 19.2.9 on this line. Next 16.4.1 includes fixes for conditional [typed-array/cache](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/security/advisories/GHSA-673q-mjm6-r5xr) and [image-optimizer](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/security/advisories/GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw) issues; their Next-server prerequisites must not be presumed for Vinext. Vite 8.1.6 covers the [Windows launch-editor fix](https://github.com/vitejs/launch-editor/security/advisories/GHSA-587h-7wmj-h5pm) and earlier [Windows file-deny bypass](https://github.com/vitejs/vite/security/advisories/GHSA-fx2h-pf6j-xcff); the latter requires an exposed development server and relevant allowed filesystem paths. Loopback test/owner operation is not proof of every hosting condition.

Plugin RSC 0.5.26 still contains a vendored 19.2.6 copy. Updating only the direct pin would be insufficient evidence. Actual no-listen Vite development resolution for both rsc/ssr server.edge and client.edge selects the direct package, and its prebundle sourcemaps resolve to package version 19.2.9. An instrumentation-only production build records 13 RSC module entries across rsc/client/ssr, all from direct 19.2.9 and none from vendor/react-server-dom. This verifies the selected modules for the current configuration, not reachability of every framework request path or removal of every installed old file. Raw ignored trace records remain in the isolated worktree; no vendor patch was needed.

Candidate audit is 52 records across 10 names (0 critical, 25 high, 19 moderate, 8 low), with exit 1 because findings remain. It is not a clean audit. The scoped residual queue is:

| Installed residual | Dependency path and next investigation |
| --- | --- |
| esbuild 0.18.20 / 0.27.3 (2 records) | Drizzle's legacy loader and Wrangler build tooling; assess compatible parent/loader updates and dev-server prerequisites. |
| ws 8.18.0 (2), undici 7.24.8 (24) | Cloudflare Vite plugin/Wrangler/Miniflare local tooling and proxy/network paths; assess compatible coordinated toolchain updates, not an app-level replacement. |
| sharp 0.34.5 (3) | Miniflare copies through Cloudflare tooling remain after the Next update; upstream ranges call for at least 0.35.5 for all listed records. Next's fix does not patch these copies. Assess image-emulation input/platform conditions and parent compatibility. |
| fast-uri 3.1.5 (10) | Webpack/AJV/schema-validation dependency graph introduced by RSC peers; inspect actual tooling invocation and compatible resolution. |
| js-yaml 4.3.1 (1), brace-expansion 1.1.18 / 5.0.9 (6), zod 4.4.3 (1) | ESLint/config/glob/hook-validation paths; review patched transitive lines without changing application runtime behavior merely to quiet aggregate counts. |
| source-map-js 1.2.1 (1) | PostCSS through Next/Vite/build tooling; investigate compatible patched lock resolution and handling of untrusted source maps. |
| image-size 2.0.2 (2) | Vinext metadata/static-image dimension parsing; inspected source reads repository image files. Registry now has 2.0.3/2.0.4, so an older unpublished-version issue is not a current blocker. Review compatible parent/transitive patch and malformed image handling. |

These are path/applicability leads, not false-positive or unreachable findings. Package-manager production/dev labels do not determine what a Vinext Worker bundles. The six-pin cohort deliberately avoids broad forced overrides; remaining tooling/decoder input risks require their own compatible proof. Future review should start with these exact paths and current upstream fixed ranges. Broad local API/browser evidence, operator promotion state and limits are in the [test report](LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md#october-24--isolated-dependency-security-cohort). Source checkpoints cannot restore owner data; no hosted source sync, deployment or real publication is part of this work.

The first organizer readiness probe preceded completion of cold dependency optimization; final checks explicitly require every route to return 200. ## Keyboard task continuity — October 24

Source bec1884 on codex/keyboard-task-focus repairs reproduced judge keyboard issues: repeated Note buttons had no bib-specific accessible name or labelled row context, and acknowledged whole-stage submission removed the focused Submit control, leaving ordinary/head focus on the document body. Note's visible text/expanded state remains, with a unique Note for bib [number] accessible name. After explicit acknowledged submission, focus moves to the existing current task region; if the last head submission replaces that region with pending review, it uses the exact current-block pending round's existing result sheet. Regions have valid heading/context labels, tabIndex=-1 and visible focus rings; no permanent tab stop, extra announcement, editor redesign or new approval action is introduced. Native next Tab reaches history for the waiting ordinary fixture and the existing exact-approval control for the ready head fixture.

Focus intent is captured before the initial read, tracked through the request and one render frame, and scoped to the same connected judge shell. A deliberate focus move prevents restoration; failed/stale operations do not focus a success state. Listener/frame cleanup runs on completion/failure/unmount, and later routes cannot receive stale focus. Routine polls and private-note typing retain their existing focus. Legacy/heat submission, score-next/missing-bib recovery, archived rendering, marks/private notes/locks/history, cutoff votes, head decisions and exact approval rules remain unchanged. Organizer native Tab/Shift+Tab/Enter/Space traversal through setup guidance and all panels passed without writes, so no organizer navigation change was made. Browser accessible-name/keyboard evidence does not establish physical-device/screen-reader conformance. Verification/limits are in LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.

## Backup and restore task recovery — October 24

Source 84b0d76 on codex/recovery-task-feedback centralizes shared stage/legacy backup and restore handlers in the existing recovery module. Actual delayed-backup and note-inclusive browser baselines reproduced enabled repeat controls, absent local progress and privacy copy that still described the default after notes were selected. Both old handlers already cleared the file input correctly; no same-file picker defect was claimed. The shared hook retains task state across Set up/Publish navigation without changing setup drafts, uses a synchronous in-flight lock and disables the actual recovery file input, note option and event-management buttons while pending. Parent shared-action busy state also disables recovery. Feedback stays beside the relevant control; restore status/error precedes its explanation so it remains visible on a phone. Keyboard file focus has a visible outline.

The current note choice has an adjacent warning and credential-exclusion statement; it is captured at request start and cannot change while pending. A verified-format/version/note-state response initiates the JSON download, with truthful Download started feedback rather than a claim the file was saved to disk. Failures and unverifiable responses remain local/retryable and never announce success. Existing restore confirmation, file reset, size limit, backup format/version, server authorization and inert-history/new-ID/link behavior remain unchanged. Cancellation sends no restore request. An interrupted response may follow a committed creation, so the message does not claim that no event exists. Consecutive retries of the exact file contents reuse the existing server creation receipt while this organizer page stays open; different contents get a fresh request identity. The receipt identity is held only in memory: reloading, closing or leaving the page is outside this retry guarantee. Successful restore retains the busy state while opening the validated new event path. No server/schema/dependency/policy or result-export change. Focused local evidence/limits and checkpoint are in LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.

## Isolated test endpoints and eight-judge rehearsal — October 24

Shared `tests/test-endpoints.ts` replaces owner-port defaults in Playwright, stage/publication/legacy helpers and direct mutation checks. Read-only defaults are isolated 3001/3110. Mutations require explicit environment URLs; browser launch requires both because page visits can create fixtures or submit admin forms. Accepted origins are root HTTP loopback URLs on their respective isolated ports, without credentials, whitespace, paths, queries or fragments. Invalid/missing mutation configuration fails before any request, including a composite helper's initial read. Guarded API requests never follow redirects; explicit manual responses remain available for assertions. The actual admin browser POST/303/GET flow remains supported. Generic errors do not echo supplied URLs or credentials. This test safeguard changes no production role/permission/API policy and does not establish process/store ownership; manifest/configuration preflight remains required.

`tests/eight-judge-browser.spec.ts` rehearses eight assigned judges across one Strictly and two J&J divisions with valid scheduled songs. Key decisions use isolated actual browser roles: missing-score recovery with No, whole-stage submission, keyboard cutoff vote, exact head approvals, both actual J&J draws saved together, complete-event request, separate website-admin release, dual-approved correction and renewed head/admin approvals. Repetitive independent judge entries use the API. Retained pages poll without forced reload for stage transitions. Independent expected values include No=0 versus blank songs, 8 observations/one per judge, locked ballots, all eight votes and correction (10 + 7 × 4.5) / 8 = 5.1875, still second place. The original Strictly 4.5000 snapshot and both J&J publications remain unchanged. Author-only notes, private-reason exclusion and original submission history are checked. This is fictional local correctness/usability evidence, not capacity, physical-device, venue, hosted or real-publication proof. No further app defect was reproduced; no product change was manufactured. Evidence and delivery state are in LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.

## Finals draw blocker guidance — October 24

A completed J&J Finals card now identifies the other unfinished division and its first missing lead. Complete [division] draw reveals every collapsed ancestor and focuses that exact lead’s dropdown; the current card offers Choose missing follow for multi-lead draws, while a single-lead card keeps the missing-lead guidance beside its dropdown without a redundant shortcut. ## Released-results comparison — October 24

Source e5dc5f8 on codex/public-release-comparison from main/041b455 adds an initially collapsed What changed disclosure after the existing result content, retaining rankings first. public-site/comparison.ts reads only canonical, identity-aligned released records from the same public event/competition. It selects the unique highest lower released revision whose release time is valid and no later than the viewed release; unreleased/skipped revisions, mismatched IDs/revisions, future records and unrelated pointers cannot provide data. A supersedes pointer grants no comparison access. Historical views compare backward, never against a newer current release. Missing/ambiguous earlier history gets a quiet unavailable message; first publication has no comparison; unchanged/context-filtered views state what has no changes.

The comparison displays stored before/after averages, places, withdrawal/advancement/identity/observation changes and optional recorded-scorecard details, without recalculation or new official results. Blank, No=0, null published values and absent cells remain distinct. Unchanged displayed averages do not hide changed marks; sub-display numeric differences show distinct full published values, and observation-only differences have an explicit fact. Entrant scope follows public identity/member links across stages; judge scope lists only that judge's changed recorded scorecards. Names/labels are escaped and display uses allowlisted public fields, never private reasons/notes or internal IDs. No schema/API/release-policy/dependency/export/hosted change.

## Private judge pending-save recovery — October 21

The owner authorized another independent local bug/usability pass, coordinated with the original project chat and preserving the owner event/live site. Started from clean main/b1af3c9 with all 150 tracked preferred/mirror files byte-identical; the original chat explicitly granted source/Git/canonical-doc/mirror ownership and retained owner-preview/walkthrough authority. All fault injection and judging mutations belong to separate synthetic 3001/3110 fixtures.

Pending conflicts previously displayed only marks/errors, leaving multiple bib/song edits hard to distinguish. Each now names the entrant/couple, competition/division, stage, heat and song, and offers Open score only when that score is available in the current active assignment. The shortcut changes the selected song and focuses the exact bib without saving or discarding anything. Explicit saved/local comparisons retain private notes. Retry is offered only when at least one edit can retry; an all-blocked queue explains that comparison is required. Keeping a local conflict is disabled for archived events or locked ballots; their pending notes remain on the device. Phone context/buttons wrap within the established theme.

The shared frontend API client converts failed connections, invalid JSON and non-object responses into concise recovery messages while preserving valid server error status/details, including the saved cell on a 409 conflict. An invalid success response becomes a retryable failure. Judge saves additionally validate the complete existing acknowledgement contract before touching either saved display or pending cache: exact round/song/bib, mutation ID, base revision plus one, mark and note. An empty/incomplete/mismatched HTTP 200 must not acknowledge the edit. Retries retain the original mutation identity and private local values; only a valid acknowledgement removes the matching pending mutation. Newer edits made while an older save is in flight retain the existing queue/version protection. No server, schema, dependency, mark calculation, role, approval or immutable-submission contract changed.

Source/tests are 4a6c8b6, developed on codex/judge-save-recovery from b1af3c9. Annotated checkpoint/local-judge-recovery-2026-10-21 preserves the verified runtime/tests and canonical closeout. Use normal protected push/fast-forward integration and verify actual remote main/checkpoint plus exact branch containment before cleanup. Preserve all previous milestones; source rollback does not restore databases. Fresh verification and preservation evidence are in LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md.

## Automatic scoring stages and judge-led submission — October 21 (local)

The owner removed the organizer's stage review-opening gate and explicitly selected automatic stage opening when prerequisites are ready. This supersedes the October 20 start/review controls described below. Saving complete Prelims preparation opens scoring automatically and freezes entries, heats, songs, panel and identities with the existing immutable versions. Incomplete preparation stays editable, with named blocker routes in Score. Setup warns before saving that a complete stage opens and freezes automatically. Existing stage-workflow events activate automatic progression from their organizer page without replacing their links, marks or immutable records.

Every judge can navigate all assigned heats and songs, review saved marks and private notes, acknowledge that their scheduled dancing has finished, and submit once per stage. No organizer action opens submission. Every eligible entrant/couple still needs at least one mark in each assigned heat; No=0 counts, song blanks remain explicit, and notes do not count. Pending saves and missing coverage block submission. Every required heat snapshot and ballot lock remains in one guarded D1 transaction with exact event/round/ballot versions and retry receipts. Submission additionally protects all songs in that judge's assignment against partnership changes, including songs left blank. Recorded observations and the exceptional Record as performed control retain their existing protection behavior.

The final required submission moves the whole stage into discussion automatically. Organizer Results holds the same read-only review/scorecards, tie decisions, correction/withdrawal workflows and exact head approval gates. Judges/head judges use their private pages for votes and head decisions. Other judges' marks remain hidden until the entire stage is submitted. Finals opens automatically after every competition's current approved advancement is available and the entire saved Finals program validates. Strictly retains approved couples. J&J still requires the actual saved lead/follow draw; no pairing is invented. If one competition is unready, the whole Finals stage waits. Saving the last required draw opens Finals; no Start action is needed. Corrections that invalidate advancement still invalidate dependent approval, and started Finals remain frozen under the existing policy.

Automatic stage opening reuses the normal stage-opening validation and atomic event-version guard after a committed setup, approval, dual-approved request or automatic-flow activation. Read-only GETs never open stages. Concurrent changes trigger bounded guarded retries; activation is repeatable without refreezing existing rounds or clearing ballots. Stage-v1 context records the automatic mode without a schema migration. Explicit automaticStages:false/manual heat-mode API compatibility supports retained workflows and isolated regressions; the user-facing automatic flow has no routine Start stage or Open review controls. Historical open-stage-review calls remain compatible but are no longer a submission prerequisite. Legacy v2 meaning and publication permissions stay unchanged.

## Stage workspace and one submission per judge per stage — October 20 (implemented locally)

The owner approved the interactive Stage workspace design and authorized local implementation: “One submission per stage, blocked if anyone is missing a score in an assigned heat. Leave the live site alone for now.” This section supersedes the earlier per-song organizer advancement/per-heat submission UI descriptions for newly opened stages. Branch: `codex/stage-submission-usability`, base `61a815c`. Hosted v4/source `da371cb` remains unchanged; this is not a deployment.

### Scoring and review boundary

Start Prelims or Finals once. All frozen assigned heats/songs become available to each judge, with no organizer Next song/Next heat actions. Judges freely select any assigned song, keep per-song private notes and autosave through the existing acknowledged pending queue. The organizer sees stage submission status, named missing heat/bib coverage, direct named private judge links and expandable live marks/submission/program details. Existing links are not regenerated.

At the stage break the organizer selects Open Prelims/Finals review and confirms that all scheduled dancing has finished. Judges can review before the break but cannot submit until this gate opens. Review lists every assigned heat and its recorded marks/notes, names missing contestants with links to their exact heat, and requires an explicit acknowledgment before Submit prelims/finals. Each eligible contestant/couple needs at least one recorded mark across the songs of each assigned heat. A score in another heat does not cover that gap. No=0 satisfies coverage; individual blanks and notes remain separate. Approved withdrawals waive only withdrawn eligibility.

The server's submit-stage action validates the current block and assigned panel, active access, exact event/round generations and versions, expected ballot versions, acknowledgment and coverage of every assigned heat. In one D1 batch it inserts the retained per-heat/per-song immutable snapshots, locks every assigned ballot and records the retry receipt. Any failed guard rolls back every insertion/lock. Earlier heat snapshots are reused, never rewritten. No new table/migration is required. The client disables submission during pending saves and checks the reviewed cell revisions/eligibility again after refresh; bookkeeping version updates alone do not force a second acknowledgment. Stale polls cannot unlock submitted ballots or regress a completed stage to dancing.

After the last required judge submits, every competition in the stage enters results discussion together. Until then other judges' marks/rankings remain hidden from judge pages; organizer-only live marks remain available. Panel cutoff voting, head deadlock decisions, exact head approval, current approved advancement, actual saved J&J Finals draws, frozen started Finals and independent publication release gates are unchanged. Genuine-error amendments/withdrawals still require both distinct authorized roles, retain previous snapshots, and invalidate affected approvals. Removing the only outstanding competition through the existing dual-approved withdrawal also permits the remaining fully submitted stage into review.

### Performance and withdrawal protection

Available songs are not automatically marked danced. The first nonblank observation (including No) records that song as performed and protects its partners, even if the score is later cleared. For an already performed song with no marks yet, the organizer uses Record as performed in the collapsed prepared heat/song list before requesting a withdrawal. This is an exceptional recordkeeping control, not routine song advancement. Opening stage review confirms the full schedule is complete and protects all its partnerships. Undanced replacement plans still require exact eligible partners and both approvals; new performed observations invalidate older plans. A database guard prevents applying a plan loaded before a concurrent observation. Changed membership after started Finals remains blocked under the existing policy.

### Information layout and preservation

Pending judge/head review duties are first; Head Judge — Name is the visible heading. Completed judging, private submitted notes and completed requests start collapsed. No score, vote, explanation, timestamp, retained version or approval record is deleted. Organizer Results keeps the selected-round ranking and review scope while Scorecards includes all competition rounds, each expandable with every heat's songs and named judge marks/means. Blanks, No=0, individual J&J roles, actual Finals couples and withdrawn records remain distinct. Earlier versions/approvals stay in Audit trail. Judge access is immediately available through named links; copying/revoking/replacing links remains in the collapsed management area. Gold hero buttons keep dark readable text on hover/focus.

### Existing events and local scope

New open-stage commands default to the stage flow. Existing in-progress heat-flow events expose Open all stage heats & songs, which preserves scores, notes, already submitted immutable heats and history while opening the remaining assigned work. Existing completed stages require no conversion. Explicit heat-mode API compatibility remains for earlier workflows/regression fixtures; submit-heat and per-song room advancement are rejected once stage submission is enabled. Legacy block/v2 data, restores and historic meanings are unchanged. No owner event is used as a mutation fixture. Local preview is 127.0.0.1:3000; isolated verification uses a separate Worker/D1 store on 3001 and a loopback fake receiver on 3110. See LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md for actual checks, limits and final mirror status.
