- Keep the existing navigation and event header. Place the Event foundation banner first, followed by Save setup, saved/unsaved status, Go to section and Event tools. Keep toolbar behavior consistent across screen sizes; allow controls to wrap.
- The banner contains Event foundation, Set up the desk once, short preparation guidance and the competition count. Sample help appears inside this banner only for the fictional sample and expands its instructions there.
- Main desktop rows are Details / Program, then Judges / Couples-or-Contestants. Stack the same cards in that order on narrow screens. All four start collapsed on both screen sizes and expand through their headings; preserve the accepted fields and controls inside. Compact closed headings retain useful summaries and actionable setup issues. Opening/collapsing or navigating sections must preserve unsaved edits. Go to section opens and scrolls to the selected card without closing other cards.
- Details contains Event name, Venue and Date.
- Program contains + Strictly / + J&J, selectable competition rows and the selected competition details. Its Prelims/Finals selector changes the view/configuration, never advances judging. Stage order is an expandable subsection. Retain existing competition editing/removal protections.
- Judges contains an add-name control, numbered editable judge list/count and visible Head judge selector/head label. Role verification and Import judges are expandable, with verification status visible while closed. Private judge links stay in Score. Preserve started-panel protections.
- Couples/Contestants follows Program selection and shows competition/division/round, entry count and roster. Strictly Prelims uses Number/Lead/Follow; J&J Prelims uses Number/Name/Role. Heat assignments and Import roster are expandable below the visible entries; keep assignment status visible. Finals follow approved advancement, with actual J&J draw controls shown when needed. Preserve approval and frozen-round protections.
- Round settings sits below the four cards, identifies the selected competition/round, and starts collapsed itself. Its three sections start collapsed on both screen sizes: Heats & songs, Judges for this round, and Finalist count. Keep counts/issues visible while closed. Group heats with their songs, optional per-song J&J partner maps and organizer-only notes; heat assignments stay under the roster with a shortcut. Round panel selection uses event judges with the head identified. Finalist count uses format-specific labels, shows capacity/approval status, works from either round and preserves renewed-approval and frozen-Finals rules. Actual J&J draw stays with the Finals roster.
- Event tools contains Copy organizer link and an Other events shortcut. Other events is a collapsed section at the bottom; its shortcut expands and scrolls to it. Preserve the existing event-management capabilities. Copy keeps the existing private-link semantics. The existing private organizer link is copied only on the explicit Copy action. The organizer home retains its saved-event picker and sample/blank creation controls. A direct /live entry has a guarded link back to that event’s organizer workspace, opening Other events there.
- The earlier phone-only Details-open default is rejected. Responsive resizing must not reset expansion or selected competition/round. Cross-visit persistence of expansion choices was not explicitly settled and must not be treated as implemented or required by this mockup.

Implementation detail: Program’s + Strictly / + J&J actions reveal division selection and the existing Add competition action, retaining duplicate checks. Only the selected competition’s editable division/removal controls are shown. The Prelims/Finals selector is limited to that competition. Judges has a name-entry form, numbered editable names and visible Head judge selection. Roster rows expose existing bib/name fields; role identity remains visible, and started rounds stay frozen. Heat assignments, imports, role verification and Stage order use native disclosures. The Heats & songs section links directly to Heat assignments. Finalist-count controls and their approval/maximum warnings remain available from either round; actual J&J pair selectors now sit in the Finals roster and retain the existing pairing handler. Legacy manual finalist selection and versioned corrections remain supported.

Implementation reuses the current setup handlers, expected-version saves, import validators, frozen-round controls and server approval/permission checks. No server, schema, dependency, scoring, publication or storage-policy change is included. Regression fixtures use isolated stores; the owner’s preview is inspected read-only. The previous walkthrough and mockup were design evidence only; fresh application evidence is recorded in LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md. Other page reviews remain pending.

This describes the inspected implementation and the owner's decisions. It is intended for people and coding assistants. Source links are relative to the application repository. ## Agreed Score layout - implemented locally

After the guided review and full mockup, the owner authorized implementing the agreed Score layout locally, preserving functionality/data and leaving the online site unchanged. This presentation pass reuses the current local/shared stage workflow. Set up remains as delivered. The original theme/colors are retained; the mockup used an approximation and does not define a replacement palette.

**Implemented top section:** keep a visible top card showing the actual current stage and next action, including competition/division/round, heat/song while running, required-judge submission status and applicable blockers. Place the competition/round view selector below it. Viewing another round must not change the running floor. The card adapts to not started, judging, review and completed states. Preserve the same layout/behavior across screen sizes and all organizer/judge permissions and action gates.

**Implemented stage preparation/controls:** beneath the view selector, show separate Prelims and Finals disclosure cards with visible status summaries. Start both collapsed on phone and desktop; the top status/action area routes to the relevant preparation or setup detail when attention is needed. Prelims details explain saved-program readiness and actionable setup blockers. Finals details explain required current head approvals and saved advancement, and retain actual J&J draw controls with Save/Discard under the relevant competition. Strictly finalists continue to follow approved advancement; never invent J&J pairs. Preserve the event-wide stage sequence and existing start requirements. Keep the primary stage start/progression action in the approved visible top area: Next song, Open heat-end lineup, waiting for named required submissions, then Next heat/division or Conclude stage and open review. Completed stages retain a compact approval summary and Results route. This changes presentation only; readiness, submissions, approval and frozen-round rules remain as implemented.

**Implemented judge access:** place one initially collapsed Judge access card below stage preparation. Its closed summary shows judge count, named head and active/revoked link counts. Expanded rows retain judge name, Head judge label, Active/Revoked access status, Copy judge link and Open judge page. Keep Revoke and Replace link inside a separate collapsed Manage link disclosure for each judge, with the existing confirmations and role/status gates. Retain one event-long private link per judge across heats and stages. Add a compact Judge access shortcut in the visible top area to open/scroll to the card before and during judging. On phone, wrap the same controls beneath each judge's name; keep the same behavior and default states. Link status indicates access, not online presence or submission progress. Judge scoring remains on private judge pages. No automatic link distribution or changes to scoring/access rules are proposed.

**Implemented submissions and coverage:** consolidate the two existing detail areas into one initially collapsed Submissions & coverage card below Judge access. Its summary explicitly identifies the actual current stage and counts received versus required judge-per-heat submissions. When expanded, separate Heat submissions from Scoring coverage. Heat submissions groups the current stage by competition/division and heat, with the current heat first when applicable; show only that round's assigned judges, Head judge labels and Received/Awaiting submission. Before a stage starts, show Not started rather than a misleading zero-of-zero ready state. Scoring coverage groups all started rounds and their assigned judges, preserving the existing cross-round information. For stage-v1, label covered entrant/couple counts accurately: at least one recorded mark across the entrant's heat songs, not every song scored. No is a recorded mark; blanks remain distinct. Legacy coverage counts remain labeled as marks rather than entrants. Full coverage does not imply a received heat submission or head approval. Keep actual current-heat blockers/named missing judges visible in the approved top area; a submission-details shortcut opens this card. Preserve last-connected-status wording on errors. This coverage card displays no private judge notes or score values; the separate Live scores card exposes organizer-only saved marks. Official results remain in Results after stage closure. Phone/desktop use the same state and controls with wrapping only.

The implementation uses the existing StageDesk action handlers, validation, expected-version saves, separate judge routes and FinalsPairings controls. ScoreProgress groups the existing submission/coverage payload without changing its calculation. Cross-round per-judge submission status remains visible separately from coverage. The four supporting cards start collapsed at every width; shortcuts open/scroll/focus without closing other cards. Resize does not reset disclosure state or drafts. The competition/round selector retains its existing view-only persistence. Current-stage counts are independent of that selection. Link Active/Revoked refers only to access. Legacy workflow handlers remain unchanged.

The full guided mockup is design history. Fresh application evidence is in LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md. No server, schema, dependencies, storage policy, scoring/approval rules, online site, deployment or publication changes are included. The owner preview uses its existing data; all mutation checks use isolated synthetic events. The existing forest/cream/gold palette, typography and button classes are reused; new CSS only controls spacing/wrapping and existing theme variables.


## Score live marks and progress - implemented locally

The completed Score layout/source/tests are committed locally as `ad684c8`, followed by documentation `463b6f6`. The live follow-up is committed locally as `3eaf572`. The owner approved live updates, superseding their initial submitted-only preference: Score shows saved marks as judging proceeds; Results remains the place for round review and final approval.

The competition/round selector now includes a progress bar labeled Heat submissions received, with a count of acknowledged judge-per-heat submissions for the selected round. Before that round starts it says Not started. Its View live scores shortcut opens an initially collapsed Live scores card directly below the selector. The actual floor and live controls remain at the top, independent of the viewed round. Prelims, Finals, Judge access and Submissions & coverage retain their agreed layout and behavior.

Live scores is read-only: heat/song selectors, entrant or couple bib/name rows and assigned judge columns. Each judge is labeled Draft or Submitted / locked for that heat. Saved draft marks can change until heat submission; submitted values come from the latest immutable heat snapshot, including a later properly approved correction. No mark editing, clearing, notes or calculated rankings appear. Explicit saved blanks, not-yet-scored cells and No / zero are distinct; future songs say Not started, and withdrawn entrants retain their marked history. The view follows the selected round's current song until the organizer chooses a song; explicit selection remains stable during polling and resizing, with a Follow current song action. The same disclosure/selector behavior works on phone and desktop, with internal horizontal table scrolling at narrow widths.

The existing refresh interval is approximately four seconds plus network time. A narrow organizer-only `liveScores` allowlist includes heat/judge identities, ballot/submission versions and saved marks for frozen heat members and visited songs. It excludes private notes and full editable ballots. Judge and head accounts do not receive this feed. Their existing stage-close access to submitted review marks is unchanged. Delayed same-event-version reads cannot roll back a newer draft or locked submission, while new event versions/generations remain authoritative. Connection errors label retained data as last-connected scores/status. Existing judge-only saves, immutable submission locks, exact approvals, corrections/withdrawals and publication contracts remain unchanged. No schema, dependency or infrastructure change was needed.

The original palette is retained. At that Score delivery, Results layout and approval behavior were unchanged; its pre-review privacy sentence was made precise (judges cannot see one another's marks during judging) to avoid contradicting the newly authorized organizer feed. Fresh evidence and limits are in LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md. All writes for verification use synthetic isolated events; the owner's data and online site are preserved.

The owner approved the Results top-section structure: selected-round status and next action stay visible, the competition/round selector sits below, and other competitions/pending work stay expandable. The complete structure is now implemented locally following explicit owner authorization. Keep the same structure on phone and desktop. Judging in progress routes to Score, provisional results identify panel/head work, and approved results route to remaining reviews, Finals preparation or event publication review as applicable.

## Results standings - implemented locally

The October 19 annotated follow-up supersedes the original always-open full standings layout. An always-visible results summary sits directly below the competition/round view selector. The owner chose Top 3 by default, with Top 5 and All display options. For each result group, include every entrant sharing the cutoff place, even when that exceeds the selected count. This selection is local view state only; it never changes finalist counts, advancement, numeric scores, live floor or approval. It survives responsive resizing and refresh polling, and resets when the Results view remounts.

The owner approved removing the duplicate Full standings section. The always-visible Results summary is the sole ranking table, with All showing every eligible entry. Each displayed entry has a View scorecard shortcut that opens and focuses its exact retained card. The footer opens Scorecards, and includes a direct withdrawn-record shortcut when applicable. The table uses forest-green circular place badges, warm table headings, distinct number/name/average columns where space permits, and the existing four-decimal averages. On narrow phones, the bib moves above the name in the same cell to keep the page within the viewport. Detailed scorecards use the same place badge treatment.

Strictly and Finals show actual couples; J&J Prelims keep separate Lead and Follow groups, with Top X applied independently to each role. Provisional results are labeled explicitly; Advancing appears only after exact head approval, and pending tie/shared-place/head-decision statuses remain beside entries. Withdrawn entrants remain outside eligible ranks and retain their detailed records at the end of Scorecards; its closed summary shows their count. Before stage review opens, Results does not display live draft rankings. The annotated online site remains unchanged; the current local scoring and privacy contracts remain authoritative.

Acknowledged/required heat-submission counts and recorded head approval details remain inside Tie decisions & approval. Removing the duplicate ranking section does not remove records or change scoring, eligibility, approvals or exact scorecard navigation.

## Results scorecards - implemented locally

The owner approved the proposed scorecard layout: a collapsed Scorecards section below the visible standings, containing one initially collapsed card per entrant/couple. Each card summary identifies place/status, bib, name(s) and the existing four-decimal overall average; J&J Prelims retain Lead/Follow identity and Finals use actual pairs. A View scorecard shortcut beside a standings entry can reveal the section and exact card without changing the selected round. Cards follow the existing standings order within each group, with unranked/withdrawn records afterward. More than one card can remain open.

An expanded card shows the assigned heat and songs as rows, assigned named judges as columns (head labeled), and only submitted marks from the selected result, including properly approved amendments. Blank / Not observed is distinct from No / zero. A footer displays each judge's existing average and recorded-mark count. Keep per-song averages/counts and the scoring legend/explanation in a small expandable Calculation details area within the card, preserving existing data without making the main standings wider. Explain that blanks are excluded and the overall average weights each judge's average equally. Do not recalculate or round source values differently, expose private notes, allow mark editing or mix the organizer live-draft feed into Results scorecards.

Phone and desktop share the same controls and initial states, wrapping names and scrolling only the scorecard table horizontally. Opening a card must not close another card or move the selected round. The owner confirmed one expandable card per entrant/couple, visible judge averages and expandable calculation details. Tie decisions, approval details and the separate audit trail were subsequently agreed. Implementation followed the complete layout review and explicit local authorization.

## Results tie decisions, approvals and audit trail - implemented locally

The owner approved one initially collapsed Tie decisions & approval section below scorecards, with a visible summary of pending decisions/current approval and a shortcut from the top status/action panel. Outstanding blockers remain visible at the top and beside affected standings entries. Expanded Prelims details show the tied entrants, available advancement slots, submitted/required panel votes and judges still needed; remaining full-panel deadlock identifies the head decision needed. Finals shows affected couples and the head's ordered/shared-place decision without changing averages. Organizer controls route to the existing private head/judge access; voting, tie decisions and exact result approval retain their existing role gates. Approval details identify competition/round, pending conditions, head name, recorded approval time and whether that approval is current. Changed results requiring renewed approval must not inherit an older approved label.

The owner also suggested incorporating the online audit trail. Read-only inspection of the existing online Results tab confirmed an AUDIT TRAIL / Revisions section and timestamped saved-revision placeholder. The reference's manual Save revision and older unlocked-ballot behavior are not adopted. The local server already retains heat-submission versions, tie decisions/votes, approval snapshots and correction/withdrawal versions, but the normal organizer view does not expose all historical records together.

The owner approved a separate initially collapsed Audit trail near the bottom of Results, defaulting to the selected competition/round with an All competitions view filter. Show newest recorded milestones first: submitted heats, recorded tie decisions, head approvals and requested/revised/cancelled/applied corrections or withdrawals. Each entry shows readable date/time, recorded actor/role where available, competition/round, plain-language action and outcome. Expand an entry for existing reasons, before/after marks, exact approval records or the retained historical result snapshot where available. Distinguish past/superseded approvals from current approval. Use existing recorded timestamps only; do not invent times or claim every draft edit was historically logged. This is a read-only presentation of retained records, requiring a narrow organizer history payload at implementation time; exclude private notes, credentials and internal identities. Historical snapshots must not replace the current standings or selected review. Leave publication delivery history in Publish. Same behavior on phone and desktop with stacked entries. The owner confirmed Tie decisions & approval stays collapsed with pending work visible at the top, and Audit trail stays separate and collapsed. The owner subsequently authorized this local implementation.

## Results corrections and withdrawals - implemented locally

The owner approved one initially collapsed Corrections & withdrawals card after Tie decisions & approval and before Audit trail. Its closed summary shows pending-request count/status; top status and the expandable other-competitions overview expose pending work and link to the exact request. Default new requests to the selected competition/round, with the affected scope explicitly labeled. Pending requests appear first when expanded, showing affected entrants, requested before/after change or withdrawal, reason and separate organizer/head approval states. Allow the organizer to revise/cancel a pending request through its own controls. Changing a request requires both roles to approve the revised exact version; requesting or a single approval never applies a change. Keep superseded/resolved records available through the audit trail and preserve existing permissions and server gates.

Request a score correction opens a focused form for submitted heat/judge, song, bib/name, recorded mark, proposed corrected mark and reason. Show a plain-language before/after preview before sending the request. Put mistaken-bib transfer in an optional expandable subsection identifying both entries and both effects; preserve existing transfer semantics and submitted-record identity. This is for genuine recording errors and does not unlock direct score editing.

Request a withdrawal offers entrant/couple withdrawal, with whole-competition withdrawal in a separate less-prominent disclosure. Show scope and reason, that fixed partners withdraw together, retained marks/history, and the actual resulting approval/eligibility requirements before requesting. For J&J Prelims, expose any required undanced-song pairing plan as expandable Review remaining pairings using named/bib choices, preserving exact validation and existing danced pairs; do not invent partners or a new changed-membership/redance policy. Unavailable operations stay blocked with a clear explanation. Keep action forms closed until selected, use the same phone/desktop behavior, and preserve drafts during responsive resizing. The owner confirmed the card stays collapsed, pending requests come first and forms/uncommon options are expandable. The complete agreed Results layout was reviewed and explicitly authorized before implementation.

## Complete agreed Results layout - implemented locally

After reviewing the complete layout, the owner explicitly authorized local implementation while preserving all functionality/data and leaving the online site unchanged. Implemented reading order: (1) visible selected competition/round status, next action and pending blockers; (2) visible competition/round view selector with collapsed other competitions/pending-work overview; (3) always-visible Top 3 / Top 5 / All results summary with scorecard and withdrawn-record shortcuts; (4) collapsed Scorecards, containing collapsed entrant/couple cards and expandable calculation details; (5) collapsed Tie decisions & approval with a useful pending/current-approval summary; (6) collapsed Corrections & withdrawals, showing pending requests first when expanded and revealing forms/uncommon options on demand; (7) separate collapsed Audit trail with selected-round default and optional all-competitions view.

Use the established forest/cream/gold theme and the same layout order, controls and disclosure defaults on phone and desktop. Wrap names/actions responsively, fit the main standings to the page, and confine wide scorecard scrolling to the card. Explicit shortcuts open/scroll/focus their exact target without closing other expanded sections. Resizing does not reset selected round, disclosure state or in-progress forms. Display selections and historical record inspection never change the live floor or current approval scope. Before the whole stage closes, show the current judging status and Score route; do not display live draft rankings as official Results. Existing role boundaries, immutable submissions, numeric scoring, history/privacy and exact approval rules remain.

The implementation uses OrganizerResults with separate standings/scorecard, request and withdrawal components. Judge/head review stays on private routes. The narrow organizer-only resultAudit payload projects already-loaded submission, vote, decision, approval and request records; no schema or database query was added. History includes readable names, recorded timestamps, retained submitted marks and approval snapshots, with no private notes or internal participant identities. When no current competition remains, Audit trail defaults to All competitions and still exposes retained history. Viewing records does not restore or replace current results. Publication delivery history remains in Publish.

Correction requests retain immutable submission identity, explicit before/after marks, optional mistaken-bib transfer and exact dual approval. Pending withdrawal revisions repopulate the named pairing controls and reset approvals through existing server behavior. Reviewed pairings display names/bibs instead of raw JSON. Visited pairings, current eligibility validation, frozen Finals and the unresolved changed-membership/redance policy remain unchanged. No numeric scoring, mutation authorization, storage or publication rule changes were made. The online reference and owner event data remain unchanged; fresh local synthetic evidence and limits are in LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md.

## Publish guided review - October 19

Following Results refinement commit `aef7668`, the owner requested reviewing Publish section by section before any application changes. Source inspection confirms the existing whole-event task card, exact prepared previews, current publication history, past versions, publication explanation and recovery controls. Accepted first section: an always-visible whole-event status/next-action card, competition count, clear organizer-versus-website-admin responsibility, and actionable blocker shortcuts with additional items expandable. Preserve state-specific Review results to publish / Send results for approval / waiting / in-progress / published behavior, archived read-only state and existing server gates. The owner approved the complete Publish layout and explicitly authorized local implementation; the accepted structure is now implemented. Do not add a per-competition publishing action or interpret navigation as publication approval. Accepted results preview/grouping: one initially collapsed card per competition in program order, with format/division, result version and actual publication status visible in the heading. Expanding a card shows all eligible final places using the Results place styling. Prelims, scorecards and scoring details stay nested and initially collapsed, with separate J&J Prelims role groups and one expandable entrant/couple scorecard. Preserve withdrawn records and public-preview privacy. Use the exact prepared publication snapshot, clearly flag changed/outdated previews, and provide a route back to Results for changes. Expanding a preview does not approve, send or select a partial event. The owner approved collapsed competition cards, final places visible when expanded, and expandable scorecards inside. Accepted history/status structure: one initially collapsed Publication history & status section below previews, with a concise current-status summary when closed. Inside, show current competition records first with competition/division, result version, actual status, Published and Last sent recorded dates; use explicit empty states. Nest Past result versions in an initially collapsed disclosure, with older versions grouped by competition newest first and each retained preview expandable. Distinguish previously published records from the current public version and never infer that every prepared or superseded version was published. Keep actionable warnings in the visible top section even when history is closed. History remains read only and uses retained records rather than a fabricated every-action audit log. The owner approved collapsed Publication history & status, current records first and past versions expandable inside. Accepted utilities: separate collapsed About publication and Data & recovery sections after history. About publication explains existing optional publication, organizer/head/admin responsibilities, renewed approvals and hosting/service limits in plain language. Data & recovery opens with Download event backup and a short explanation; optional inclusion of private notes remains off by default in expandable Backup options. Restore from backup is expandable and explicitly creates a new event/new links, with retained results historical rather than active submissions or approvals. Legacy device backup/history and restored historical records remain expandable. Archive/delete controls move into an initially collapsed Archive or delete event subsection with existing confirmations, role checks and archived-only deletion preserved. All controls use the same layout/behavior across phone and desktop. The owner approved both sections collapsed, backup visible when Data & recovery opens, and other controls expandable underneath. All sections were agreed individually, and the owner approved the complete layout for local implementation. Final reading order: (1) always-visible whole-event status, next action and blocker shortcuts, with additional blockers expandable; (2) collapsed competition preview cards in program order, final places visible when opened and detailed scorecards expandable inside; (3) collapsed Publication history & status with current records first and nested past versions; (4) collapsed About publication; (5) collapsed Data & recovery with visible backup action when opened and nested options/restore/history/archive-delete. Preserve the existing cream/forest/gold palette, accessible keyboard expansion, independent open cards and responsive state preservation. Same controls and behavior on phone and desktop. The complete layout is implemented locally. Shared stage/legacy recovery presentation reuses the existing backup/restore/archive/delete handlers and gates. Publication history groups older preparations by competition, newest preparation first (including multiple preparations of the same result version); previously published records retain original dates and never-published records are explicit. Archived events retain their read-only top status. Source checks and synthetic verification are recorded in LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md; the online site and owner data remain unchanged. Next: owner review of the local Publish page.

## Quick context

- Owner: Wesley Coffman. The tool conducts Strictly couples and Jack & Jill competitions for the local community.
- Organizer navigation is Set up → Score → Results → Publish, with the original theme, cards, bibs and collapsed setup sections. Set up is the initial organizer screen. Organizer Score contains room controls and submission/coverage progress; Results contains review, amendments and withdrawals. Judge score entry stays on private links. Imports remain in Set up; private judge access is in organizer Score and Data & recovery is in Publish.
- Each private judge/head route has one Score page with assigned ballots, own notes and relevant review duties inline. Server role gates and explicit payload allowlists prevent organizer-page access or organizer configuration disclosure through judge links. Organizer distribution/testing access to judge links remains intentional.
- Configure the finalist count from either Prelims or Finals setup. Exact head-approved advancement automatically populates unopened Finals: Strictly couples copy directly; J&J advancing leads/follows require the actual draw pairs before judging. Cutoff votes and frozen Finals protections remain.
- New events use stage-v1: acknowledged heat snapshots, all-required-judge coverage/quorum, whole-stage review, equal judge means, full-panel advancement votes, head deadlock/finals placement decisions and exact head approvals. Organizer alone initiates/revises/cancels versioned corrections and withdrawals; distinct organizer and head explicitly approve the same exact version.
- `/` is the working organizer desk, with real Set up → Score → Results → Publish tabs. It reopens the saved D1 event directly; new visitors get a fictional ready-to-start sample. Judges use separate private links. Legacy v2 bytes/scores/revisions remain untouched on startup; only preparation may be copied, with fresh IDs and no score conversion. Legacy backup/history/restore is nested in Data & recovery.
- React 19 + TypeScript, Vinext/Vite App Router and logical D1 `DB`; four-second polling and revision/CAS guards remain. Operational data, local fake publisher storage and owner administration are separate. Publication is optional and never a scoring dependency.
- Only completed competitions with current matching head-approved advancement/Finals can prepare an immutable public snapshot. Organizer reviews the exact complete-event preview and sends it for approval; the separate results website’s authenticated admin approves and publishes the event with one top action. Public judge names, marks, blanks and counts are included; private notes, participant IDs, access credentials, setup notes and internal reasons are excluded.
- The separate local public site has Winners, Leaderboard, contestant/pair and named judge cards, Under review notices, immutable numbered revisions, stable current URLs and Superseded history. A durable outbox retries authorized local deliveries; the receiver validates current source, scope, ordering and independent numeric expectations before its atomic current-pointer change.
- Publication suspension/shutdown retain operational judging and recovery. Post-Finals changed-membership replacement/redance, reinstatement/substitutes and legacy-only publication remain deferred; invalidate/block rather than invent policy.
- Branding is the authorized Interskate/Lewisville community. A verified original 150×150 profile emblem is used only at a small size; cream/forest/copper public styling is an interpretation, with system/Georgia fonts and two credited original community photos. The judging template is preserved.
- Physical devices, venue Wi-Fi, hosted D1/auth/domain/provisioning/migrations/deployment, production capacity/uptime and real publication remain unperformed. There is no service worker or guaranteed offline reload.
- Read AGENTS.md and task-specific sections; inspect branch/status and compare mirror before edits/sync. Preserve existing/uncommitted data/work. Local success does not authorize public release or purchases.

## Next batch: local organizer usability redesign

Status: **implemented and verified locally on `codex/organizer-usability-redesign`, October 11**. Implementation/test commit `240dcc3` starts from verified checkpoint `714d2be88261adf2b34e0afa2b93e8975147b0c3`; the checkpoint branch/tag remain preserved. Initial parity covered all 120 tracked files. The final 17 affected source/test/doc files are guarded and byte-matched with the mirror. Fresh verification passed 33 units, 37 serialized compiled API checks, all 25 browser scenarios and a final nine-scenario recheck after small wording changes, lint, TypeScript, production build and whitespace checks. Phone/desktop inspection, restart preservation, three SQLite integrity checks and absence of both server keys from 24 client artifacts also passed. See the test report for exact commands, failures corrected and limits.

The implemented view selector is event-scoped session state: changing it does not start a stage, alter setup or widen approval scope; polling does not move the selected review. Manual setup selection carries into Run/Results, while unsaved drafts retain their existing explicit Save/discard and stale-source rules. Run distinguishes event-wide room order from selected view, counts only the heat's assigned panel, labels disconnected data as last connected status and keeps completed stages compact. Organizer Results shows one round while named pending duties and the collapsed full overview preserve access to other rounds. Head/judge action permissions remain unchanged. Blocker shortcuts reveal and focus the exact setup or request section. Judge ballots now identify their format as well as division/round.

October 11 scoped review: an unresolved cutoff tie says **Panel votes needed** while votes remain, then **Head decision needed** for a full-panel deadlock, then **Head review needed** after the head resolves it. Both the selected context and collapsed overview use this status. Existing head-access navigation reveals/focuses the private links without changing the event. Legacy preparation summaries say **Finalists selected manually** rather than claiming advancement approval from saved entrant IDs; stage-v1 exact advancement status and all underlying approval gates remain unchanged. Both defects were reproduced before fixing them. Fresh verification and local delivery are recorded in the test report.

Publish leads with readiness and every competition's exact snapshot, then status/history and recovery. Sent-for-approval and confirmed released states remain distinct. No validated public results URL exists in the current LiveView contract, so no View published results link is invented. The older remote site informed visual hierarchy only and was not changed. No API/schema/scoring/approval/dependency change was needed.

October 11 browser-reviewed Publish clarification: the owner chose one scorecard per entrant/couple. Each expandable card keeps place, bib/name and four-decimal average in its summary, then displays assigned songs as rows and named judges as columns, with each judge's recorded average and observation count below. Blank is explicitly Not observed and remains distinct from No; no numeric value is recalculated by this presentation. J&J Prelims remain grouped as Leads/Follows and Finals identify the actual couple. Withdrawn observations, exact snapshots, older versions and scoring explanations remain available. Tables scroll within their own keyboard-focusable region on narrow screens. Publication history uses competition cards with human status, Result version, Published and Last sent; visible dates use browser locale/timezone while time elements retain original ISO timestamps. The existing review and send actions remain gated as before; the current task now clearly distinguishes review, Send results for approval, waiting for website admin, finishing publication and already published. A released event explicitly says no further publishing action is needed and offers Review judging results. No public URL is inferred. Source/tests are local `37a0581`, with fresh evidence in the test report.

The owner requested a guided review of the other sections, starting with Results. Results is open on the retained local event; questions about selected-round hierarchy and disclosure of per-song averages have been presented. Results, Run and Set up have not been changed by this Publish follow-up. The October 17 overall-layout authorization above is now the next step; these unanswered section-specific questions remain for the later guided review.

The next concrete step is the explicitly requested new implementation chat for the approved overall layout match, then owner comparison at `http://127.0.0.1:3000/` and guided section review. Preserve the owner event. The October 11 delivery remains a completed historical batch; the October 17 layout work is not yet implemented. Hosted deployment, physical phones/venue networks, capacity and guaranteed offline reload remain unperformed; post-Finals changed-membership replacement/redance remains undefined and blocked.

### Checkpoint, working copies and delivery

1. Read AGENTS.md, this section, relevant implemented contracts, and the latest LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md. Inspect Git status and confirm the local checkpoint tag resolves before branching. Preferred repository: `C:\Users\Iam\Projects\Country Dance Judging App\app`; Documents is a source mirror inside a different parent repository. Compare affected files before every sync. Do not commit the parent workspace.
2. Start `codex/organizer-usability-redesign` from `checkpoint/local-before-usability-redesign-2026-10-11`. If a branch with that name already exists, inspect it before reuse; never reset unrelated work. Preserve the checkpoint tag/branch. The previous application-only baseline is `122cd23`; the new tag also includes this handoff documentation.
3. Make reviewable local commits for the coherent redesign and its meaningful regression coverage, followed by updated canonical documentation. Do not push, merge the checkpoint branch, deploy, synchronize hosted source, register a site, provision storage, purchase anything or publish real results. The remote site must remain exactly as is.
4. Use the running previews where available: organizer `http://127.0.0.1:3000/`, separate results `http://127.0.0.1:3100/`, website admin `http://127.0.0.1:3100/admin`. These are local services; separate public/admin hosting does not exist. Avoid restarting the receiver unnecessarily because its admin sessions are held in memory. Isolate all fixtures from the owner's browser and local stores.

### Observed friction to address

- Remote navigation explains each step with subtitles; local navigation has only Set up / Run / Results / Publish. Run gives little indication of its purpose.
- Local setup is compact but its collapsed sections require opening to assess readiness. Preserve collapsed defaults while adding useful, truthful summaries.
- In the inspected completed sample, Run shows Finals review alongside disabled Start prelims stage / Start finals stage controls and approved labels. Earlier completed actions distract from current work, and the review message needs a direct route to the relevant results.
- Results stacks Prelims and Finals for every competition into one long page. The currently relevant competition/round and outstanding tasks are difficult to locate.
- Publish places Data & recovery above the publication task. Maintenance and historical detail should follow the current task.
- Extra approval steps reflect real role boundaries. Explain the responsible person and next action in plain language rather than implying the organizer can complete another role's decision.

### Experience and layout requirements

| Surface | Required next-batch behavior | Preserved constraints |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shared organizer shell | Retain the numbered forest sidebar/phone rail, cream cards and bib treatment. Restore concise subtitles: Set up — Event & entries; Run — Judges & rounds; Results — Review & advance; Publish — Send approved results. Maintain an unmistakable active tab and comfortable spacing. Use one consistent event/competition/round/status header where relevant. | No second navigation system, basic/advanced mode, new dashboard or duplicate workflow. Keep text usable at phone widths; accessible names remain descriptive even when subtitles are visually reduced. |
| Competition and round context | Make the selected competition and round visible in Run/Results; provide a compact selector or overview using existing program data. Preserve deliberate selection between tabs where meaningful. Pending work elsewhere remains visible through named status/actions. | Display selection must never change the actual running heat/stage, eligibility, saved setup or approval scope. If the active stage is event-wide, clearly distinguish its scope from the selected competition view. Whole-event Publish always covers the entire event. Do not silently jump while someone reviews or edits. |
| Set up | Keep sections initially collapsed. Show concise summaries drawn from actual setup, such as assigned judge count, entrant count and finalist count. Separate configured counts from validated readiness. A blocker shortcut opens/focuses the relevant section. Keep explicit Save and clear saved/unsaved feedback. Move lengthy sample instructions into short contextual help. | Preserve common local/shared editor, imports, keyboard access, drafts across navigation, cancel/discard behavior and server version guards. Initial organizer entry remains Set up; no forced wizard. Do not auto-save, create events or change counts/pairings as a side effect of navigation. |
| Run | Lead with the current stage/heat, truthful submission progress and one primary actionable next step. Completed stages become compact statuses with a review link instead of prominent disabled start buttons. Judge access is easy to reach below the main action. Show Finals draw controls when needed, keeping the existing Save pairings flow. | Progress denominators must come from the relevant assigned judges/heat/stage, not an assumed panel of three. Distinguish submitted, acknowledged, missing coverage and disconnected/unknown states. Keep required detail reachable. Never enable Start when drafts, count, pairing or approval rules block it. |
| Results | Default to the relevant competition/round, with a compact overview that makes other competitions and pending duties discoverable. Lead with placement table and review status/actions; keep the exact subject of any approval immediately visible. Provide a direct route back to the next round or Publish when eligible. | Retain all results, both J&J roles, ties, withdrawn/archived labels, submitted marks, scorecards, requests and history. Organizer sees status/routes; only authorized judges/head see their existing vote/decision/approval controls. No cross-competition stale-selection approvals. |
| Publish | Put readiness and the exact complete-event preview before Data & recovery. Use the existing review/send flow with a clear Send results for approval action and explanation of website-admin approval. After confirmed release, make View published results prominent when an existing validated results URL is available. Keep maintenance and publication history below the main task. | Preserve explicit exact-version review, all-competition scope, independent website-admin release and failure/retry semantics. Do not infer Published from delivery alone, invent a public URL, add an organizer admin credential screen or bypass re-review after source changes. |
| Private judge/head pages | Keep assigned name, current ballot and existing Score flow central. Bring a required review/correction duty forward when relevant; preserve submitted ballot access and existing head duties. Limit changes to coherence with the organizer redesign and verified usability needs. | Organizer/judge separation, note privacy, named confirmation, immutable submitted ballots and exact permission gates remain. Do not rebuild the judging engine or expand this into an unrelated public-site redesign. |

The main action area should state where the user is, what needs attention, who is responsible and the next available action. Example copy is illustrative, not hard-coded logic: "2 of 3 judges submitted — waiting for Robin's ballot" with Open judge links; after all applicable submissions, "Prelims ready for review — head judge approval needed before Finals" with Review prelims. Handle pluralization, actual assignments, multi-heat stages and unavailable data accurately. Required pending requests must remain visible even when historical details are collapsed.

### State and navigation acceptance matrix

| Scenario | What the user should see and be able to do |
| --- | --- |
| Blank/incomplete setup | A concise explanation of the actual missing requirement and a shortcut to its field/section; no misleading Ready state. Empty program remains usable. |
| Valid preparation | A clear Start action for the eligible stage; configured competition and actual stage scope are apparent. |
| Active judging with missing coverage/submissions | Correct scoped progress and relevant judge links; no premature official placements or stage transition. |
| Stage awaiting review | Review action leads to the exact competition/stage and pending duties. Organizer cannot perform head-only work. |
| Pending correction / cutoff tie / head decision | Named blocker and correct-role route remain visible; cancelled/applied requests do not incorrectly block approval. |
| Prelims approved / Finals not ready | Approved status plus the actual count or pairing requirement. Shortcuts preserve selection and drafts; unsaved/stale pairing guards remain. |
| Finals active | The active round is obvious; completed Prelims controls are quiet; frozen rosters/pairings are not editable. |
| Finals approved / another competition incomplete | Results are easy to locate; Publish names the unfinished competition and routes to it without offering partial publication. |
| Event ready / awaiting website admin / released / invalidated | Each state has accurate copy, one useful primary action where available, exact preview and accessible history. Changed sources require renewed review. |
| Archived event | Clear read-only status; prior ballots/results/history accessible; no active mutation controls. |
| Navigation, reload and concurrent edit | User can return to the intended competition/round; no cross-event selection leak, dropped drafts, silently changed action target or bypass of stale-version checks. |

### Implementation sequence and source routing

1. Inspect current desktop and 360/390px layouts with isolated synthetic sessions. Compare the preserved remote interface read-only. Capture focused baseline evidence; never use owner event data for test mutations.
2. Implement shell hierarchy, descriptive navigation and display selection/context first. Likely entry points: `components/live/shell.tsx`, `organizer-home.tsx`, `desk.tsx`, `stage-desk.tsx` and `app/globals.css`. Verify actual responsibilities before editing.
3. Add truthful collapsed summaries in `setup.tsx` / `setup-section.tsx` and state-specific Run presentation in the existing desk components. Reuse `finals-pairings.tsx`; preserve setup/CAS handlers and underlying domain rules.
4. Focus Results using `results.tsx` / `stage-review.tsx`, then reorder/clarify `publication.tsx` using the existing readiness helper. Keep current role-aware shared rendering; avoid parallel implementations or a speculative abstraction layer.
5. Complete browser walkthrough and regression checks, inspect final phone/desktop visuals, and correct observed issues. Update these three canonical Markdown documents with implemented behavior, exact evidence, local commits and remaining limitations. Sync only affected source/test/doc files after guarded comparisons. Keep the new local preview available for owner review.

No scoring, API, schema, approval or authorization changes are planned. If a presentation defect requires a small underlying fix, reproduce it, preserve the contract, add appropriate regression coverage and document it. Material policy ambiguity stays deferred; in particular do not invent post-Finals replacement/redance rules. Do not add dependencies, services or hosted work to solve layout concerns.

### Verification and definition of done

- When a change needs browser coverage, add focused checks for truthful state/action transitions, retained selection, direct blocker navigation, keyboard/focus, phone layout and access to hidden details as affected. Use semantic controls; do not weaken existing assertions just because labels moved. Avoid tests that merely mirror component structure or exact styling.
- At the end of a major workflow delivery, walk one complete isolated fictional event through setup, Prelims, separate judge submissions, head review/advancement, actual J&J draw, Finals and complete-event publication using the local fake receiver and separate website-admin/public contexts. Include missing submission, pending correction, tie, unfinished competition and archived-state cases, plus existing count-cycle/stale-draft regressions as applicable. Assert independent numerical outcomes and role gates. Do not repeat this complete rehearsal for each small change.
- Follow [Verification cadence](#verification-cadence): use fast checks as needed during small changes, then applicable full unit/live API/browser suites, lint, TypeScript, production build and `git diff --check` at the major delivery checkpoint. Use an isolated compiled Worker/D1 store and receiver for publication state changes. Report exact passed/failed/unperformed checks and distinguish historical evidence from new verification. No real external sends.
- At 360, 390 and desktop widths, primary context/action is readable, labels/focus work, controls do not overlap, main placement columns fit, and intentional detailed scorecard scrolling remains understandable. Confirm collapsed setup defaults and reachable historical detail.
- The task is complete when existing supported behavior is retained and every matrix state communicates current context, responsibility and a useful next action without requiring the organizer to interpret internal mechanics. Keep unresolved visual or workflow issues explicit; automated checks do not establish ease of use or venue/hosted readiness on their own.

Read AGENTS.md, PROJECT_SPEC.md revision 1.34 (Online layout match and new-chat handoff) and the latest LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md. Verify branch/status and preferred/mirror parity. Use the online site only as a read-only visual reference; match its layout and labels while keeping all added local functionality in appropriate subsections. Start with the overall layout, show the local result, then conduct guided section review. Preserve owner data, scoring/role/approval/privacy/history/frozen-Finals contracts and checkpoint `714d2be`. Use proportionate verification and update relevant Markdown at each stopping point. No push, hosted sync, deployment, provisioning, real publication or purchase.

## Product and workflow

### Established owner decisions

The site should be usable by friends running community competitions and presentable as a public portfolio project. The owner accepted public access and the current URL, declined custom in-app authentication, and explicitly preferred sidebar navigation because it communicates competition flow. Preserve these choices unless a new request changes them. Low stakes justify simplicity, but scores and saved work should remain predictable.

### Implemented local/demo surfaces

| Surface | Current behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Set up | One common editor; collapsed sections, event/judges, program, required panels, finalists/pairs, roster/heats and rotations. Inline CSV examples/imports; explicit Save persists drafts. Imports stay here; judge access/room controls live in organizer Score and Data & recovery lives in Publish. |
| Organizer Score | Start stage, next song/heat, lineup and conclude/open review, plus room/submission/coverage progress and collapsed private judge access (Copy/Open and link management). No judge mark entry, judge submission, panel votes or head approval actions. Concluding a stage opens Results. |
| Results | Main standings show place, entrant and average; observation counts and explicit blanks remain in detailed scorecards. Organizer recording-error requests and competition/entrant withdrawals share a collapsed Corrections & withdrawals section. Read-only scorecards, tie/approval details and retained Audit trail have separate collapsed sections. Head approvals/decisions remain on the private judge page. |
| Publish | Collapsed Data & recovery (operational/v2 backups, restores, retained history and archive controls), plus exact public snapshot preview and organizer approval request. Disabled preparation explains missing stages/submissions/head approvals or changed finalists and offers navigation to the next step. One top action reviews all competitions, followed by Send results for approval above their final placements. Status explains website-admin approval, sending pending or Published. Detailed scorecards and prior versions are collapsed; the judging app has no admin credential or approval screen. No result export or Google publishing controls. |
| Organizer home / legacy recovery | Browser remembers private D1 access separately from backups. Fresh visitor gets sample preparation; existing event reopens. Read-only v2 archive and wrapped/plain restore sit in Data & recovery. Restore opens a separate setup copy without importing legacy scores as stage marks. |

Preparation removal edits the current D1 event; the separately retained legacy archive, including earlier removed-competition history, is unchanged. Started event assignments stay frozen; use documented review requests rather than deleting score history. Restoring device data replaces that device event after confirmation. Shared restore creates a fresh preparation event with new links and inert imported history. Link codes remain separate from backups. There is no automatic migration of v2 scores into stage averages.

## Scoring contract

This section describes retained legacy v2 semantics, used for recovery/history rather than new organizer score entry. Authoritative implementation: [lib/scoring.ts](lib/scoring.ts). Examples: [tests/scoring.test.ts](tests/scoring.test.ts).

| Mark | Numeric value | Counts as filled? |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| `Yes` | 10 | Yes |
| `A1` | 4.5 | Yes |
| `A2` | 4.3 | Yes |
| `No` | 0 | Yes |
| empty string | contributes 0 to helper total | No |

Scores are sums, not averages or ordinal judge rankings. An entry is eligible for standings only when at least one judge exists and every current judge has a nonempty mark. The ballot is complete only when `entries × judges > 0` and all required marks are selected.

Sort completed entries by descending total. Totals differing by less than 0.0001 share place. Use competition ranking: totals 30, 30, 28 produce places 1, 1, 3. Entry ID orders rows with exactly equal totals for display; it must not award one tied entry a better place. Strictly has one Couples group. Jack & Jill has separate Leads and Follows groups (with a defensive Unassigneds group).

Historically, saving a v2 revision required the whole ballot to be complete; the current interface only reads those retained revisions. It appends an ID, ISO timestamp, sequential label, and calculated result rows. It does not lock marks, advance a round, or create an authenticated audit record. The current UI lists revision timestamps/counts; it does not provide full historical-row browsing. CSV/print use current results, including completed entries in a still-provisional ballot.

Legacy mode has no callbacks or automatic advancement. No skating/relative-placement algorithm, judge quotas or official sanctioning-body compliance is implemented. Confirm competition rules before changing the established mark values or ranking semantics.

## Data and persistence

Legacy types/history fixture: [lib/domain.ts](lib/domain.ts). Organizer home: [organizer-home.tsx](components/live/organizer-home.tsx); fictional stage preparation and fresh identity copies: [sample-setup.ts](lib/sample-setup.ts).

| Object | Fields / ownership |
| --- | --- |
| `AppState` | `version: 2`, `event`, `judges[]`, `competitions[]`, `activeCompetitionId`, `publisherUrl`; optional `demoSetup`, `removedCompetitions` |
| Event details | `name`, `venue`, `date` |
| Judge | `id`, `name`; event-wide |
| Competition | `id`, `name`, `format`, `division`, `round`, `entries[]`, `scores`, `revisions[]` |
| Entry | `id`, numeric `number`; Strictly uses `lead`/`follow`; Jack & Jill uses `name`/`role` |
| Scores | Nested lookup `scores[entryId][judgeId] = Mark` |
| Result row | Entry ID/number, primary/secondary names, group, place, tied flag, total, complete flag |
| Revision | ID, creation time, label, snapshot of result rows |

The organizer home reads `country-dance-judging:v2` without rewriting it. Invalid legacy data stays untouched; when no saved D1 event exists it blocks automatic setup copying until recovery. Save setup edits the current D1 event with an explicit version guard and does not rewrite the legacy archive. Historical publisher URL/key fields are inert and no key control or outgoing publisher remains in the UI. New backups clear publisherUrl; the separate historical publisher-key is not exported. Storage is per browser profile and origin, not per signed-in user.

The main organizer workspace uses the same authoritative D1 engine as private event URLs. A new browser gets its own sample event and private organizer binding; an explicit organizer URL can reopen an existing event. `country-dance-judging:rehearsal` stores the current access/creation nonce; `country-dance-judging:organizer-history` stores earlier private event links, separately from backups. Legacy scores stay in their original origin-local archive; a setup-only copy does not migrate marks, revisions or approvals. Changing browser/device/hostname does not transfer those device records. Browser access keys are not encrypted; keep organizer URLs private.

### JSON backup contract

Current exports wrap the event as follows:

```json
{
  "format": "country-dance-judging-backup",
  "backupVersion": 1,
  "exportedAt": "ISO timestamp",
  "event": { "version": 2, "publisherUrl": "", "...": "remaining AppState fields" }
}
```

The example is a shape illustration, not an importable backup. Full ballots, event details, judges, competitions, and revisions are exported. The publishing key is separate and not exported. Restore accepts the wrapped format and older raw v2 state objects; the inert historical publisher URL is excluded from new backups and no publisher key is exported. New fields must not accidentally introduce credentials into exports.

`isAppState` only checks a few top-level properties and the presence of the active competition (or empty competitions with an empty active ID). It is not deep schema validation. Restore currently does not enforce `backupVersion`, inspect all nested fields, or deeply validate nested values. Device and shared restore now cap input at 1.5 MB. Do not describe it as fully validated input.

### CSV contract

Setup imports are confined to preparation and use inline examples rather than downloadable templates. Strictly: `Number,Lead,Follow`; J&J: `Number,Name,Role`; judges: `Name,ParticipantId`. Judge imports validate distinct participant identities and cannot replace a panel after a round starts. Validated imports change the editor draft only; Save setup is explicit. Roster imports use existing deep setup checks and respect frozen started rounds. The parser supports quoted commas, line breaks and escaped quotes. Result CSV/Excel/PDF/scorecard/history-download/print features are removed; JSON recovery remains separately labeled.

### Event-day usage

Run deliberately created private organizer/judge routes, keep organizer and distribution links separately, and make operational JSON backups before recovery operations. Judge pending edits remain attached to their original song and retry online. A checked heat submission becomes immutable after named confirmation; every active entrant needs an observation from each assigned judge. Explicit No is coverage and zero; a blank is not observed. Publication service availability has no bearing on operational judging. First load/reload still requires the operational service; no guaranteed offline installation exists.

## Inert Google compatibility source

[Do_Post.gs](google-apps-script/Do_Post.gs) and its historical setup README remain as compatibility source/history only. No organizer/public route, key/URL editor, send control or result export invokes them. Editing this source does not deploy Apps Script. No Google request was made. New publication is the separately owner-controlled, local-only workflow described below; real external publishing requires separate authorization.

## Usability contract

The intended audience includes nontechnical organizers, dancers and judges who may not know the judging process. Every screen must make the next step, responsible person and outcome clear in ordinary language. Keep a single useful primary action near the top, with the information being approved immediately below it. State actionable blockers next to disabled controls and offer a shortcut to the relevant section. Show names/bibs, placements, required approvals and concise status; collapse full marks/calculations, technical metadata, history and uncommon maintenance. Group related corrections/withdrawals and event-wide publication tasks. Do not remove evidence, privacy or exact approval checks to simplify the display. Assess new features for control placement, understandable wording, keyboard access and narrow-screen fit. AGENTS.md makes this a standing implementation requirement; no external documentation upload is required or authorized by these local changes.

The local cleanup applies this to stage review: each competition shows its head approval action, reason for any blocker, panel vote and head tie decision before its placements. Pending recording-error corrections block both Prelims and Finals approval; cancelled/applied requests no longer block the button, matching the existing server gate. A Review pending corrections shortcut scrolls to and focuses the exact request. Pending request cards precede resolved cards, retain entrant/couple names, bibs, original/intended marks and approval status, and collapse requester identities, version/timestamps and detailed pairing records. Private submitted notes remain author-only. Archived event votes and correction/withdrawal management are read only in the UI as well as on the server. Completed-stage judge review hides an empty song selector and explains that the submitted scores are locked. Phone result tables retain place, entrant/bib and average together without horizontal scrolling; full calculations remain expandable. Public/admin styling shares the same readable hierarchy without changing approval actions or receiver sessions.

## Visual design

Authoritative styles: [app/globals.css](app/globals.css). Direction: warm paper panels, deep forest navigation, copper/gold accents, serif headings and practical sans-serif controls. Preserve the competition workflow as the primary surface.

| Token | Value / role |

| `--paper`, `--panel` | `#f5f1e8`, `#fffdf8`; backgrounds |

Organizer desktop sidebar is 218 px and sticky. At 720 px or narrower it becomes an 88 px vertical rail; at 420 px it is 74 px. Do not revert it to horizontal top navigation. At 1,100 px setup cards stack; compact Results tables fit the available width. Organizer desktop actions/fields use the reference's compact 38/40 px geometry and become at least 44 px on phones. The separate private judge ballot retains its 44 px mark buttons and larger phone text; the older combined 880 px ballot table is not the organizer workflow. Wide detailed publication scorecards scroll only within their labeled, keyboard-focusable region.

Preserve `aria-current="step"`, `aria-pressed` marks, associated labels, visible focus, status messages, and reduced-motion styles. A dialog role exists but comprehensive focus trapping/Escape handling has not been verified. Print rules hide navigation/control surfaces and emphasize the result sheet.

Social preview metadata is in `app/layout.tsx`. `/og` serves a JPEG encoded in `lib/social-image.ts`; avoid loading that base64 blob during normal code searches. The preview currently says “Offline-ready scoring,” which overstates offline reload support. The image has a long immutable cache lifetime at a stable path, so image changes may need cache/version treatment.

### Commands and checks

From the app root, use Node >=22.13 and pnpm:

```sh
pnpm install
pnpm dev
pnpm test
pnpm lint
pnpm exec tsc --noEmit
pnpm build
```

The package scripts invoke Vinext, not the standard Next CLI. Respect the lockfile and dependency security policy. `pnpm-workspace.yaml` now uses explicit boolean `allowBuilds` for the four existing native build dependencies. The earlier unresolved strings emitted ignored-build warnings. Inspect policy deliberately if install is blocked. A historical successful direct build does not prove a clean install is reproducible.

The August release had seven scoring/CSV tests. Current shared work also has focused unit, actual local D1/API and browser suites. Select checks using the cadence below; the full suite is a major delivery checkpoint. Tests do not confirm physical-phone/venue behavior, hosted resources or real Apps Script delivery.

### Verification cadence

Effective 2026-10-15, at the owner's request, use fast feedback during development and reserve full end-to-end testing for the end of major deliveries.

- **Small features and fixes:** choose relevant local unit/regression tests, explicit TypeScript checking, lint and other inexpensive checks when needed for the change. `pnpm test` is available for the fast unit suite; a single unit file can use `node --experimental-strip-types --test tests/<file>.test.ts`. Use `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint` and `git diff --check` as applicable. Preserve scoring cases and add meaningful focused coverage for new data-handling behavior or reproduced bugs; do not create tests for trivial copy/style edits or checks that simply mirror the implementation.
- **Behavior needing integration evidence:** use the smallest relevant API or browser scenario when unit/type/lint checks cannot establish the changed behavior. Focus browser files with `pnpm exec playwright test tests/<file>-browser.spec.ts`. Check affected scoring, role/approval gates, persistence and history promptly rather than waiting to discover a regression at delivery. This does not require a complete end-to-end rehearsal for a small change.
- **Major feature or coherent delivery batch:** once implementation is stable, run the applicable full unit and live API suites, the full end-to-end browser suite, lint, explicit typecheck, production build and whitespace checks before declaring the major delivery complete. Exercise the complete isolated mock workflow when the delivery affects that workflow. Accumulate small changes into this checkpoint instead of rerunning the full suite after each edit or small commit.
- **Reruns and documentation:** after checks pass, rerun or broaden them only when new changes, a failure or an unresolved concern warrants it. Documentation-only changes need factual, link/anchor and diff checks; they do not need application tests or a build. Record the checks selected, actual results and deferred full-suite coverage in LOCAL_TEST_REPORT.md without presenting older evidence as a fresh run.

Test publication against the local synthetic receiver only and preserve owner data and isolated role sessions at every verification level. Do not claim a successful TypeScript check based solely on a Vite build. This cadence changes when checks run; scoring, permission, approval, history and frozen-Finals acceptance requirements remain.

### Hosting and public sharing

For shared local development, run `pnpm install`, `pnpm build`, `pnpm db:local`, then `pnpm dev`. `pnpm db:generate` appends Drizzle SQL after schema changes. The migration helper records local applied file hashes under ignored `.wrangler/`; do not edit already applied SQL/meta or replay migrations blindly. Use `.wrangler/state` for both preview and local migrations.

After verifying the task-owned process/configuration/store manifest, set `LIVE_TEST_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3001` and `LOCAL_TEST_RECEIVER_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3110` explicitly. `pnpm test:live` runs the actual HTTP/D1 suite; `pnpm test:browser` uses isolated headless Microsoft Edge contexts (Windows default). Test helpers reject owner ports 3000/3100 and unsafe origins before mutation, and Playwright requires both endpoints before launch. The fast `pnpm test` suite needs no running service. Set Playwright's browser channel to an installed supported browser on other hosts. Outputs in `test-results/` and local D1/cache folders remain ignored; they contain synthetic rehearsal data and are not source artifacts.

Public URL: [country-dance-judging.wes6969.chatgpt.site](https://country-dance-judging.wes6969.chatgpt.site/).

The project ID is in `.openai/hosting.json`; reuse it. Current confirmed public release is [Sites v5/source45c9682](#milestone-live-judging-release--october-26), October 26, 2026. Historical v3/v4 evidence remains below and in Git; do not treat it as current deployment state. Verify live metadata when a new deployment task needs fresh information.

GitHub and Sites source hosting are separate destinations. Pushing GitHub alone does not publish the website. Follow the currently installed Sites hosting guidance: validate, push exact source to the site's repository, package that build, save/deploy the version, and verify completion/access. Never persist the temporary source token in a remote URL or this document. A documentation update alone does not require redeployment.

| Area | Current limit |

| Offline operation | Online retry/pending cache works; no service worker, guaranteed offline first load/reload or measured extended venue outage. |

Two original public community photos (640×449 and 640×480) are selected for modest web display; no high-resolution/print suitability or paid font license is claimed. |
